Foundations for operator algebraic tricategories
Giovanni Ferrer

Abstract
An operator algebraic tricategory is a higher categorical analogue of an operator algebra. For algebraic tricategories, Gordon, Power, and Street proved that every algebraic tricategory is equivalent to a Gray-category, a result later refined by Gurski. We adapt this result to the context of functional analysis, showing that every operator algebraic tricategory is equivalent to an operator Gray-category. We then categorify the Gelfand-Naimark theorem for operator algebras, inductively proving that every (small) operator algebraic tricategory is equivalent to a concrete operator Gray-category. We also provide several examples of interest for operator algebraic tricategories.



1
Introduction

Operator algebras were first conceived in the context of quantum mechanics emerging as concretely C*-algebras and von Neumann algebras of operators acting on a Hilbert space
[
GN43
]

[
Seg47
]

[
MvN36
]
.
We have since seen a movement throughout mathematics of categorifying structures and their results.
Higher operator algebraic categories arise in the study of subfactor theory where, in particular, operator algebras with their bimodules and intertwiners form an operator 2-category.
Picking a particular operator algebra
A
, i.e a C*/W*-algebra, functors from a unitary tensor category
{\mathcal{C}}
into
\End(A)
in this operator 2-category are then quantum categorical/non-invertible symmetries.

Recently, even higher (braided) operator algebraic categories have emerged as
invariants of operator algebraic objects, like that of the categorified Connes’
\chi(M)

[
CJP21
]
.
Moreover, operator algebraic 2-categories are expected to form an operator algebraic 3-category
[
CP22
]
.
Higher operator algebraic categories have also seen important applications in topological order.
Starting in a 2+1D topological order, that is a unitary modular tensor category
{\mathcal{C}}
, the topological domain walls for
{\mathcal{C}}
are classified by the fusion 2-category
\operatorname{Mod}({\mathcal{C}})

[
KK12
]
.
Condensed matter physics is manifestly unitary, where time reversal symmetry is taking the Hermitian adjoint.
We thus desire a definition of unitarity for a fusion 2-category, which can also be viewed as a 3-category with 1 object.
Furthermore, physicists want to go even higher — 3+1D topological order is expected to be described by a unitary modular fusion 2-category, for which there is not yet a formal definition in the literature
[
JF22
]
.

Algebraic models for higher categories do, however, pose difficulties due to their large amount of constraint data.
Coherence theorems allow us to consider semi-strictified models of algebraic higher categories while retaining full generality.
These semistrictified models are then easier to work with as they require less data.
Notably, Gordon, Power, and Street proved that every algebraic tricategory is equivalent to a Gray-category, the strictest model of a general algebraic tricategory
[
GPS95
]
. The definition of an algebraic category together with this theorem were later refined by Gurski
[
Gur13
]
.

Operator algebraic tricategories are tricategorical analogues of operator algebras, for which one would desire an analoguous coherence theorem.
However, constructions from ordinary category theory may fail the principle of equivalence
[
nLa23
]
for
{\dagger}
-categories, as noted in
[
Pen20
]
for dual functors on a unitary multifusion category.
Moreover, classical results may run into complications when equipping algebraic objects with topologies. For example, the algebraic tensor product of C*-algebras may be equipped with many possible norms, including the maximal and minimal tensor product norms. Out of these only the maximal tensor product satisfies hom-tensor adjunction, while the minimal tensor product is more concrete and proves to be useful in practice.

In this article, we present a definition for operator algebraic tricategories and operator Gray-categories, as well as provide examples of interest.
This requires us to construct an operator algebraic Gray tensor product, which uses substantial functional analysis, e.g., the notion of biduals for C*-algebras, and its categorification to C*-categories and C*-2-categories.
We then adapt the coherence theorem for algebraic tricategories to the context of functional analysis, obtaining our following main result.

Theorem A
.

Every operator algebraic tricategory is equivalent to an operator Gray-category.

Operator categories, and in particular operator algebras, are said to be concrete when they are made up of Hilbert spaces and operators between them.
We inductively extend this notion to higher operator categories, saying that an operator
n
-category is concrete when it is made up of concrete operator
(n-1)
-categories and higher operators between them.
As a corollary of our main result, we provide the following categorification of the Gelfand-Naimark theorem.

Theorem B
.

Every (small) operator algebtraic tricategory is equivalent to a concrete operator Gray-category.

This articles also provides a first stepping stone for future projects in 3-categorical unitary linear algebra.
In particular, there is relevant interest in a theory of higher Hilbert spaces and manifestly unitary higher condensation, as well as a definition and classification of unitary dual 2-functors on a unitary multifusion 2-category.

The structure of this article is as follows. In Section §
2
, we construct the operator algebraic versions of the Gray-tensor product. In Section §
3
, we define operator 3-categories, operator Gray-categories, and state our main result. We provide background needed for Section §
2
in Appendix §
A
, as well as defer some details from this section to Appendix §
B
. We then construct in detail the operator algebraic Morita 3-categories
\CHaus
and
\Meas
in Appendix §
C
. Finally, we provide a detailed account of the Yoneda embedding for so-called cubical operator algebraic 3-categories in Appendix §
D
.

Acknowledgements.

The author would like to thank David Penneys and Corey Jones for many useful conversations.
This work was supported by NSF DMS grants 1654159 and 2154389.

2
2-categorical results

We mimic this section’s results one categorical dimension down in Appendix §
A
, and defer some details about operator 2-categories to Appendix §
B
.

2.1
Operator 2-categories

We will follow
[
CHPJP22
]
in the following treatment of operator algebraic bicategories, except that we do not assume such 2-categories are unitarily Cauchy complete.

Definition 2.1
.

A C*-2-category consists of a (linear, weak) 2-category
({\mathcal{C}},\ccirc,1,\alpha,\lambda,\rho)
together with:

(
{\dagger}
)

a dagger structure, i.e. an involutive contravariant conjugate-linear 2-functor
{\dagger}:\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\operatorname{op}}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
, which is the identity on objects and 1-morphisms, such that the associator, left unitor, and right unitor

\displaystyle\alpha_{X,Y,Z}

\displaystyle\colon X\ccirc_{B}(Y\ccirc_{C}Z)\tto(X\ccirc_{B}Y)\ccirc_{C}Z,

\displaystyle\lambda_{X}

\displaystyle\colon\mathrm{id}_{A}\ccirc_{A}X\tto X,

\displaystyle\rho_{X}

\displaystyle\colon X\ccirc_{B}\mathrm{id}_{B}\tto X

are unitary (
u^{\dagger}=u^{-1}
) for composable 1-morphisms
A\xrightarrow{X}B\xrightarrow{Y}C\xrightarrow{Z}D
in
{\mathcal{C}}
and the composition
\ccirc
of 1-morphisms in
{\mathcal{C}}
is
{\dagger}
-preserving,

(C*)

the hom-category
{\mathcal{C}}(A\to B)
is a C*-category for every pair of objects
A,B\in{\mathcal{C}}
.

Furthermore, we say that a C*-2-category is a W*-2-category when:

(W*1)

every hom-category
{\mathcal{C}}(A\to B)
is a W*-category,

(W*2)

1-composition
\ccirc
is separately normal (weak*-continuous).

In
[
CHPJP22
]
it is shown that (W*2) is equivalent to the following condition:

(W*2
′
)

For every object
A\in{\mathcal{C}}
, the pre-composition and post-composition maps
\mathrm{id}_{A}\ccirc_{A}-
and
-\ccirc_{A}\mathrm{id}_{A}
are normal.

We will use the terms operator algebraic bicategory or operator 2-category to refer to C*/W*-2-categories.
Moreover, we say that an operator 2-category is strict if the underlying 2-category is strict. Notice we use the terms 2-category and bicategory interchangeably, specifying whenever they are strict.

Example 2.2
.

Given an operator 2-category
{\mathcal{A}}
, we may produce related operator 2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\operatorname{\ccirc\!p}}}
and
{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\operatorname{op}}}
given by reversing the direction of 1-morphisms and 2-morphisms respectively.

Definition 2.3
.

A
{\dagger}
-2-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
between C*-2-categories is a (linear) 2-functor
(F,F^{2},F^{0})
such that

(
{\dagger}
)

F
is dagger-preserving, i.e
F(x^{\dagger})=F(x)^{\dagger}
for every 2-morphism
x
in
{\mathcal{C}}
,

(coh)

the compositor and unitor

\displaystyle F^{2}_{X,Y}

\displaystyle\colon F(X)\ccirc_{F(B)}F(Y)\tto F(X\ccirc_{B}Y),

\displaystyle F^{0}_{A}

\displaystyle\colon\mathrm{id}_{F(A)}\tto F(\mathrm{id}_{A}),

are unitary for
A\xrightarrow{X}B\xrightarrow{Y}C
in
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Furthermore, we say that a
{\dagger}
-2-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
between W*-2-categories is normal when

(W*)

it is normal (weak*-continuous) on hom-spaces
{\mathcal{C}}(X\tto Y)\to{\mathcal{D}}(F(X)\tto F(Y))
.

Moreover, we say that a (normal)
{\dagger}
-2-functor is strict if the underlying 2-functor is strict.

Definition 2.4
.

A
{\dagger}
-2-natural transformation
\alpha\colon F\tto G
between (normal)
{\dagger}
-2-functors is a 2-natural transformation
(\alpha,\alpha^{n})
such that

(coh)

The naturator

\alpha^{n}_{X}\colon F(X)\ccirc_{F(B)}\alpha_{B}\tto\alpha_{A}\ccirc_{G(A)}G(X)

is a unitary for every 1-morphism
A\xrightarrow{X}B
in
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Definition 2.5
.

A uniformly bounded modification
m\colon\alpha\ttto\beta
between
{\dagger}
-2-natural transformations is a modification
m=(m_{A}\colon\alpha_{A}\tto\beta_{A})_{A\in{\mathcal{C}}}
such that

\|m\|\coloneqq\sup_{A\in{\mathcal{C}}}\|m_{A}\|_{{\mathcal{D}}}<\infty.

Example 2.6
.

We denote the strict C*-2-category of C*-categories,
{\dagger}
-functors, and uniformly bounded natural transformations by
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}
.
Similarly, we denote the W*-2-category of W*-categories, normal
{\dagger}
-functors and uniformly bounded natural transformation by
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}
.

Example 2.7
.

In Proposition 2.13 of
[
CP22
]
, it is show that for C*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}
, there is a C*-2-category
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}({\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}})
of
{\dagger}
-2-functors from
{\mathcal{A}}
to
{\mathcal{B}}
,
{\dagger}
-2-natural transformations, and uniformly bounded modifications.
Moreover,
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}({\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}})
is strict whenever
{\mathcal{B}}
is strict.
There is an analogous W*-2-category
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}({\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}})
of normal
{\dagger}
-2-functors between W*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}
.

Definition 2.8
.

A
{\dagger}
-functor
F\in{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}({\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}})
is said to be a
{\dagger}
-equivalence if it is an equivalence of the underlying 2-categories. This is equivalent to
F
being unitarily essentially surjective on objects and 1-morphisms, and fully faithful on 2-morphisms.

2.2
Coherence and concreteness for operator algebraic bicategories

For algebraic 2-categories, it is well-known that every 2-category is equivalent to a strict category. This coherence theorem can be easily adapted to operator 2-categories as follows.

Theorem 2.9

(Coherence for operator 2-categories)
.

Every operator 2-category is (unitarily) equivalent to a strict operator 2-category.
In particular, every C*-2-category
{\mathcal{A}}
admits a monic
1
1

1

By monic, we mean injective on objects, 1-morphisms, and 2-morphisms.
One can easily show that the image of
{\mathcal{A}}
under such a
{\dagger}
-2-functor into a strict C*-2-category forms a (strict) C*-2-subcategory which is equivalent to
{\mathcal{A}}
.

{\dagger}
-2-functor into a strict C*-2-category afforded by the Yoneda embedding:

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\operatorname{\ccirc\!p}}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}).

In fact, there is a monic
{\dagger}
-2-functor
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}
given on objects by

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}(B)=\coprod_{A\in{\mathcal{A}}}\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A}_{B}=\coprod_{A\in{\mathcal{A}}}{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B).

When
{\mathcal{A}}
is a W*-2-category, the Yoneda embedding lands in the strict W*-2-category
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\operatorname{op}}}\to{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat})
.

For operator 1-categories, it is also well-known that every small operator 1-category can be concretely realized as a category of Hilbert spaces and operators between them
[
GLR85
]
. One can categorify this Gelfand-Naimark-Segal construction to the level of operator 2-categories, obtaining the following result.

Theorem 2.10

(Concreteness for operator 2-categories)
.

For a small operator 2-category
{\mathcal{A}}
, there exists a universal representation

\Upsilon\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathsf{Hilb^{W^{*}}},

where
\mathsf{Hilb^{W^{*}}}
is the strict W*-2-category of
all W*-subcategories of
\mathsf{Hilb}
, the W*-category of Hilbert spaces.
Moreover, the image of
\Upsilon
forms a strict operator 2-category
\GNS({\mathcal{A}})
which is equivalent to
{\mathcal{A}}
.

We provide the details of these results in Appendix §
B.1
.

2.3
Local tensor product for operator algebraic bicategories

In this section, we use the tensor product of operator categories to obtain a local tensor product of operator 2-categories.

Definition 2.11
.

Given C*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, we define the
C*-local tensor product

{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\blacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
by:

(0)

Objects are tuples of objects
(A_{1},A_{2})\in{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
;

(hom)

Hom-spaces are given by:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\blacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\big((A_{1},A_{2})\to(A_{1},B_{2})\big)\coloneqq{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(A_{1}\to B_{1})\maxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(A_{2}\to B_{2}),

where we are using the maximal tensor product of C*-categories (see Appendix
A.2
).

(
\ccirc
)

Composition
\ccirc
of 1-morphisms is given pointwise and 1-composition
\ccirc
of 2-morphisms is determined using the universal property of
\maxtimes
. In particular

(x\otimes y)\ccirc(x^{\prime}\otimes y^{\prime})=(x\ccirc x^{\prime})\otimes(y\ccirc y^{\prime}),

whenever it makes sense.

(
\circ
)

Composition
\circ
of 2-morphism is defined similarly.

(coh)

Constraint data is given by simple tensors of constraint data in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
.
These are indeed natural by the bilinearity and bicontinuity of
\ccirc
and
\circ
.

Similarly, when
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
are W*-2-categories, we define the W*-2-category
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
by replacing
\maxtimes
with W*-tensor product
\Wmaxtimes
(see Appendix
A.4
).

Remark 2.12
.

When
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
are strict operator 2-categories, their local tensor product is also strict.

2.4
C*-Gray tensor product

In this section, we adapt
[
Gur13
, §3]
to the operator algebraic setting.

Definition 2.13
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, and
{\mathcal{B}}
be strict C*-2-categories. A
{\dagger}
-2-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
is
cubical
if it is strictly identity-preserving and the following condition holds:

(ø)

If
(X_{1},X_{2})
and
(Y_{1},Y_{2})
are composable 1-morphisms in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
such that
Y_{2}
or
X_{1}
is an identity, then the compositor 2-morphism

F(X_{1},X_{2})\ccirc F(Y_{1},Y_{2})\Rightarrow F\big((X_{1},X_{2})\ccirc(Y_{1},Y_{2})\big).

is an identity.

When
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
,
{\mathcal{B}}
are W*, we say that a
{\dagger}
-2-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
is a
separately normal
cubical functor if it is both normal and cubical. In particular, a cubical functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
extends to a separately normal cubical functor only when
\alpha^{\lambda}_{i}\to\alpha_{i}
weak* in
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
for
i=1,2
implies

F(\alpha^{\lambda}_{1}\otimes\alpha_{2})\to F(\alpha_{1}\otimes\alpha_{2})\text{ and }F(\alpha_{1}\otimes\alpha^{\lambda}_{2})\to F(\alpha_{1}\otimes\alpha_{2})\text{ weak* in }{\mathcal{B}}.

Proposition 2.14
.

The data of a (separately normal) cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
is determined uniquely by:

(1)

For each object
A_{1}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
, a strict (normal)
{\dagger}
-2-functor
F_{A_{1}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
;

(2)

For each object
A_{2}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, a strict (normal)
{\dagger}
-2-functor
F_{A_{2}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{B}}
;

such that

F_{A_{1}}(A_{2})=F_{A_{2}}(A_{1})\coloneqq F(A_{1},A_{2});

(\Sigma)

For each pair of 1-morphisms
X_{i}\colon A_{i}\to A_{i}^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
for
i=1,2,
an “interchanger” unitary 2-morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(A_{1},A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(A_{1},A_{2}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{A_{2}}(X_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{A_{1}}(X_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma_{X_{1},X_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{A_{2}^{\prime}}(X_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{A_{1}^{\prime}}(X_{2})}

which is an identity 2-morphism whenever
X_{1}
or
X_{2}
is an identity 1-morphism;

satisfying the following three axioms:

(\Sigma 1)

Naturality:
For each pair of 2-morphisms
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}
in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
respectively

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

Proof.

First suppose we have a (separately normal) cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor
F
. For an object
A_{1}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
we define

\displaystyle F_{A_{1}}(A_{2})

\displaystyle\coloneqq F(A_{1},A_{2}),

\displaystyle F_{A_{1}}(X)

\displaystyle\coloneqq F(\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}},X),

\displaystyle F_{A_{1}}(\alpha)

\displaystyle\coloneqq F(\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}}}\otimes\alpha).

Then
F_{A_{1}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
strictly preserves identity 1-morphisms since
F
does. Moreover,

F_{A_{1}}(Y_{1}\ccirc Y_{2})=F(\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}},Y_{1}\ccirc Y_{2})\underset{\left(\mbox{{\char 28\relax}}\right)}{=}F(\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}},Y_{1})\ccirc F(\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}},Y_{2})=F_{A_{1}}(Y_{1})\ccirc F_{A_{1}}(Y_{2}),

so
F_{A_{1}}
is a (normal) strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor. We define
F_{A_{2}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{B}}
similarly for an object
A_{2}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
. We define the unitary
\Sigma
as follows:

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

which is an identity 2-morphism when
X_{1}
or
X_{2}
is an identity by the cubicality (ø) of
F
again. Axiom (
\Sigma 1
) follows by the naturality of the compositor
F^{2}
, and axioms
(\Sigma 2)
and
(\Sigma 3)
follow by the associativity axiom
F^{2}
satisfies.

Conversely, suppose we are given such collections of (normal)
{\dagger}
-2-functors and unitaries
\Sigma
. We construct
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
as follows.

(0)

For objects,
F(A_{1},A_{2})\coloneqq F_{A_{1}}(A_{2})=F_{A_{2}}(A_{1})
;

(
hom
)

We first define
F_{\mathsf{hom}}:{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(A_{1}\to A_{1}^{\prime})\times{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(A_{2}\to A_{2}^{\prime})\to{\mathcal{B}}\big((F(A_{1},A_{2})\to F(A_{1}^{\prime},A^{\prime}_{2})\big)
by

\displaystyle F(X_{1},X_{2})

\displaystyle\coloneqq F_{A_{2}}(X_{1})\ccirc F_{A^{\prime}_{1}}(X_{2})

\displaystyle F(\alpha_{1}\otimes\alpha_{2})

\displaystyle\coloneqq F_{A_{2}}(\alpha_{1})\ccirc F_{A^{\prime}_{1}}(\alpha_{2}).

Since
F_{\mathsf{hom}}
is (separately normal)
{\dagger}
-bilinear and
{\mathcal{B}}\big((F(A_{1},A_{2})\to F(A_{1}^{\prime},A^{\prime}_{2})\big)
is C*/W*, we may uniquely extend
F_{\mathsf{hom}}
to
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(A_{1}\to A_{1}^{\prime})\maxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(A_{2}\to A_{2}^{\prime})={\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\big((A_{1},A_{2})\to(A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2}^{\prime})\big)
by the universal property of the maximal C*-tensor product
\maxtimes
(see Appendix
A.2
).

We set the unitor
F^{0}
to be identity and define the compositor component

F^{2}\colon F(X_{1},X_{2})\ccirc F(X^{\prime}_{1},X^{\prime}_{2})\Rightarrow F(X_{1}\ccirc X^{\prime}_{1},X_{2}\ccirc X^{\prime}_{2})

for
A_{i}\xRightarrow{X_{i}}A^{\prime}_{i}\xRightarrow{X^{\prime}_{i}}A^{\prime\prime}_{i}
in
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
to be

F_{A_{2}}(X_{1})\ccirc F_{A_{1}^{\prime}}(X_{2})\ccirc F_{A^{\prime}_{2}}(X^{\prime}_{1})\ccirc F_{A^{\prime\prime}_{2}}(X^{\prime}_{2})\xRightarrow{\mathrm{id}\ccirc\Sigma_{X_{2},X^{\prime}_{1}}\ccirc\mathrm{id}}F_{A_{2}}(X_{1})\ccirc F_{A_{2}}(X^{\prime}_{1})\ccirc F_{A^{\prime\prime}_{1}}(X_{2})\ccirc F_{A^{\prime\prime}_{2}}(X^{\prime}_{2}).

The naturality of
F^{2}
is guaranteed by
(\Sigma 1)
and the associativity axiom is given by
(\Sigma 2)
and
(\Sigma 3)
. Finally,
F
is cubical since
\Sigma_{X_{1},X^{\prime}_{2}}
is trivial whenever
X_{1}
or
X^{\prime}_{2}
is the identity.
∎

We now explicitly construct the Gray tensor product for strict C*-
2
-categories.

Definition 2.15
.

The algebraic Gray tensor product
{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{B}}
of strict C*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}
is composed of tuples of objects
(A,B)\in{\mathcal{A}}\times{\mathcal{B}}
. The 1-morphisms of
{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{B}}
are produced by two kinds of generators:

•

(X,\mathrm{id}_{B}):(A,B)\to(A^{\prime},B)
for
X\colon A\to A^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{A}}
,

•

(\mathrm{id}_{A},Y):(A,B^{\prime})\to(A,B^{\prime})
for
Y:B\to B^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{B}}
.

The 1-morphisms in
{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{B}}
are equivalence classes of composable strings of generators. The equivalence relation is the smallest such that:

•

(X,\mathrm{id}_{B})(X^{\prime},\mathrm{id}_{B})\thicksim(X\ccirc X^{\prime},\mathrm{id}_{B})
for
A\xrightarrow{X}A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{X^{\prime}}A^{\prime\prime}
in
X
and
B\in{\mathcal{B}}
,

•

(\mathrm{id}_{A},Y)(\mathrm{id}_{A},Y^{\prime})\thicksim(\mathrm{id}_{A},Y\ccirc Y^{\prime})
for
A\in X
and
B\xrightarrow{Y}B^{\prime}\xrightarrow{Y^{\prime}}B^{\prime\prime}
in
Y
,

•

If
W\thicksim W^{\prime}
, then
WV\thicksim W^{\prime}V
and
VW\thicksim VW^{\prime}
whenever they make sense.

We define the composition
\ccirc
of 1-morphisms to be string concatenation. Notice
W\thicksim W^{\prime}
only when
W,W^{\prime}
have the same source and target, and
(\mathrm{id}_{A},\mathrm{id}_{B})=\mathrm{id}_{(A,B)}
.

The 2-morphisms of
{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{B}}
are generated by three kinds of 2-morphisms:

•

x\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{B}}:(X,\mathrm{id}_{B})\Rightarrow(X^{\prime},\mathrm{id}_{B})
for
x:X\Rightarrow X^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{A}}
and
B\in{\mathcal{B}}
,

•

\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{A}}\otimes y:(\mathrm{id}_{A},Y)\Rightarrow(\mathrm{id}_{A},Y^{\prime})
for
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
and
y:Y\Rightarrow Y^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{B}}
,

•

\Sigma_{X,Y}:(X,\mathrm{id}_{B})\ccirc(\mathrm{id}_{A},Y)\xRightarrow{\thicksim}(\mathrm{id}_{A},Y)\ccirc(X,\mathrm{id}_{B})
for 1-morphisms
X\colon A\to A^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{A}}
and
Y\colon B\to B^{\prime}
in
Y
, such that
\Sigma_{\mathrm{id}_{A},Y}=\mathrm{id}_{(\mathrm{id}_{A},Y)}
and
\Sigma_{X,\mathrm{id}_{B}}=\mathrm{id}_{(X,\mathrm{id}_{B})}
.

We will omit the subscripts on
\mathrm{id}
’s for simplicity. The 2-morphisms in
{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{B}}
include equivalence classes of strings of formal
\ccirc
-composites of generators. The equivalence relation is first defined horizontally (in terms of
\ccirc
-composites), then vertically (as strings). We first define
\thicksim
as the smallest equivalence relation such that

•

(x\otimes\mathrm{id})\ccirc(x^{\prime}\otimes\mathrm{id})\thicksim(x\ccirc x^{\prime})\otimes\mathrm{id}
and
(\mathrm{id}\otimes y)\ccirc(\mathrm{id}\otimes y^{\prime})\thicksim\mathrm{id}\otimes(y\ccirc y^{\prime})
,

•

If
s\thicksim s^{\prime}
then
s\ccirc t\thicksim s^{\prime}\ccirc t
and
t\ccirc s\thicksim t\ccirc s^{\prime}
whenever they make sense.

Notice
s\thicksim s^{\prime}
only when
s
and
s^{\prime}
have the same source and target morphisms. In what follows, we will denote the equivalence class of
s
by
[s]
. A 2-morphism in
{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{B}}
is then an equivalence class of formal linear combinations of vertically composable strings
\sum_{k}\lambda_{k}[w_{1,k}]\cdots[w_{n,k}]
, where the equivalence relation is the smallest such that:

•

\lambda[x\otimes\mathrm{id}]+[\widetilde{x}\otimes\mathrm{id}]\thicksim[(\lambda x+\widetilde{x})\otimes\mathrm{id}]
and
\lambda[\mathrm{id}\otimes y]+[\mathrm{id}\otimes\widetilde{y}]\thicksim[\mathrm{id}\otimes(\lambda y+\widetilde{y})]
for
\lambda\in{\mathbb{C}}
,

•

[x\otimes\mathrm{id}][x^{\prime}\otimes\mathrm{id}]\thicksim[(x\circ x^{\prime})\otimes\mathrm{id}]
,
and

[\mathrm{id}\otimes y][\mathrm{id}\otimes y^{\prime}]\thicksim[\mathrm{id}\otimes(y\circ y^{\prime})]

(
\Sigma
1)

[(\mathrm{id}\otimes y)\ccirc(x\otimes\mathrm{id})][\Sigma_{X,Y}]\thicksim[\Sigma_{X^{\prime},Y^{\prime}}][(x\otimes\mathrm{id})\ccirc(\mathrm{id}\otimes y)]

(
\Sigma
2)

[(\mathrm{id}_{X^{\prime}}\otimes\mathrm{id})\ccirc\Sigma_{X,Y}][\Sigma_{X^{\prime},Y}\ccirc(\mathrm{id}_{X}\otimes\mathrm{id})]\thicksim[\Sigma_{X\ccirc X^{\prime},Y}]

(
\Sigma
3)

[\Sigma_{X,Y^{\prime}}\ccirc(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{Y})][(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{Y^{\prime}})\ccirc\Sigma_{X,Y}]\thicksim[\Sigma_{X,Y\ccirc Y^{\prime}}]

•

For general 2-morphisms
w,v
,

(w+\widetilde{w})v\thicksim wv+\widetilde{w}v
, and
v(w+\widetilde{w})\thicksim vw+v\widetilde{w}
whenever they make sense, and similar relations for
\ccirc
; and

•

if
w\thicksim w^{\prime},
then

wv\thicksim w^{\prime}v
,
vw\thicksim vw^{\prime}
, and
\lambda w+v\thicksim\lambda w^{\prime}+v
for
\lambda\in{\mathbb{C}}
whenever they make sense.

Vertical composition
\circ
of 2-morphisms is given by the bilinear extension of concatenation of strings.
For horizontal composition of strings
w,v
, we can always express
w
and
w^{\prime}
by the sum of strings
[w_{1,k}]\cdots[w_{n,k}]
and
[v_{1,k}]\cdots[v_{n,k}]
of the same length (by adding identities) and then define

w\ccirc v=\sum_{k,\ell}[w_{1,k}\ccirc v_{1,\ell}]\cdots[w_{n,k}\ccirc v_{n,\ell}].

We define
{\dagger}
on generator 2-morphisms as follows:

•

(x\otimes\mathrm{id})^{\dagger}=x^{\dagger}\otimes\mathrm{id}
,

•

(\mathrm{id}\otimes y)^{\dagger}=\mathrm{id}\otimes y^{\dagger}
,

•

\Sigma_{X,Y}^{\dagger}=\Sigma_{X,Y}^{-1}
.

We then extend
{\dagger}
to sums, tensors, and composites by:

•

(\sum_{k}\lambda_{k}[w_{k}])^{\dagger}=\sum\overline{\lambda}_{k}[w_{k}]^{\dagger}

•

(w\circ v)^{\dagger}=v^{\dagger}\circ w^{\dagger}

•

(w\ccirc v)^{\dagger}=w^{\dagger}\ccirc v^{\dagger}
.

We then define the C*-Gray tensor product
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
to be the completion of
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
on each hom-space with respect to the following maximal C*-norm

\|w\|_{\mu}\coloneqq\sup\left\{\|F(w)\|\,\middle|\,F\colon{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}\text{ is a }{\dagger}\text{-2-functor}\right\},

after quotienting out by 2-morphisms
w
such that
\|w\|_{\mu}=0
.

Remark 2.16
.

There indeed exists a
{\dagger}
-2-functor

F\colon{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}.

For example, we may use the universal representations
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}_{\mathcal{A}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}
and
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}_{\mathcal{B}}\colon{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}
in Theorem
2.9
to determine a strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor

(0)

on objects by

(A,B)\mapsto\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}_{\mathcal{A}}(A)\maxtimes\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}_{\mathcal{B}}(B);

(1)

on 1-morphisms by

\displaystyle(X,\mathrm{id})

\displaystyle\mapsto\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}_{\mathcal{A}}(X)\maxtimes\mathrm{id},

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id},Y)

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathrm{id}\maxtimes\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}_{\mathcal{B}}(Y);

(2)

on 2-morphisms by

\displaystyle x\otimes\mathrm{id}

\displaystyle\mapsto\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}_{\mathcal{A}}(x)\maxtimes\mathrm{id},

\displaystyle\mathrm{id}\otimes y

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathrm{id}\maxtimes\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}_{B}(y),

\displaystyle\Sigma_{X,Y}

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathrm{id}_{\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}_{\mathcal{A}}(X)\maxtimes\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}_{\mathcal{B}}(Y)}.

Remark 2.17
.

We expect there to exist a “minimal” Gray-tensor product
{\mathcal{A}}\underset{\min}{\boxtimes}{\mathcal{B}}
of strict C*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}
, given by completing
{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{B}}
via a monic
{\dagger}
-2-functor

{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}.

The existence of such a monic representation would imply that there do not exist negligible 2-morphisms in
{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{B}}
. However, this will not be necessary for our main coherence and concreteness results.

Remark 2.18
.

For each object
B\in{\mathcal{B}}
, there is an organic strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor

-\boxtimes B:{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{B}}

given by

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\mapsto(A,B),

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle\mapsto(X,\mathrm{id}_{B}),

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\mapsto x\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{B}},

on objects, 1-morphisms, and 2-morphisms in
{\mathcal{A}}
respectively.
Similarly, each object
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
induces a strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor

A\boxtimes-\colon{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{B}}.

Proposition 2.19
.

For strict C*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}
,
\|\cdot\|_{\mu}
is a norm on each hom-space of
{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{B}}
. Furthermore,
{\mathcal{A}}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{B}}
is a strict C*-2-category.

Proof.

A simple verification reveals that
\|\cdot\|_{\mu}
is a C*-norm, possibly taking value
\infty
. For example,

\|w\|_{\mu}=\sup_{F}\|F(w)\|=\sup_{F}\|F(w)F(w)^{\dagger}\|^{1/2}=\sup_{F}\|F(ww^{\dagger})\|^{1/2}=\|ww^{\dagger}\|_{\mu}^{1/2}

for every 2-morphism
w
in
{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{B}}
.
Furthermore,
\|\cdot\|_{\mu}
is sub-cross by the naturality and unitality of compositors. Indeed, if
\sigma\colon\phantom{}_{A}X_{B}\to\phantom{}_{A}Y_{B}
and
\sigma^{\prime}\colon\phantom{}_{B}X^{\prime}_{C}\to\phantom{}_{B}Y^{\prime}_{C}
, then

\|\sigma\ccirc\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\mu}=\sup_{F}\|F(\sigma\ccirc\sigma^{\prime})\|=\sup_{F}\|F^{2}(F\sigma\ccirc F\sigma^{\prime})F^{-2}\|\leq\sup_{F}\|F\sigma\ccirc F\sigma^{\prime}\|\leq\sup_{F}\|F\sigma\|\|F\sigma^{\prime}\|\leq\|\sigma\|_{\mu}\|\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\mu}.

Thus, to prove
\|\cdot\|_{\mu}<\infty
on each hom-set, it suffices to verify that this is the case for generator 2-morphisms
\alpha\otimes\mathrm{id}
,
\mathrm{id}\otimes\beta
, and
\Sigma_{f,g}
.
First observe that, for every object
B\in{\mathcal{B}}
and 2-morphism
\alpha
in
{\mathcal{A}}
, we have

\|F(\alpha\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{B}})\|=\|F\circ(-\boxtimes B)(\alpha)\|\leq\|\alpha\|\quad\text{for every }F:{\mathcal{A}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}.

Therefore
\|\alpha\otimes\mathrm{id}\|_{\mu}\leq\|\alpha\|
.
Similarly, one shows
\|\mathrm{id}\otimes\beta\|_{\mu}\leq\|\beta\|
.
Finally, each
\|\Sigma_{f,g}\|_{\mu}=1
since
\Sigma_{f,g}
is unitary and
{\dagger}
-2-functors preserve unitaries.
Therefore
\|\cdot\|_{\mu}
is a norm on each hom-space.
It remains to show that each hom-category

{\mathfrak{H}}\coloneqq{\mathcal{A}}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{B}}\big((A_{1},A_{2})\to(B_{1},B_{2})\big)

satisfies the positivity condition required of C*-categories (see Appendix
A.1
).
We already know that each endomorphism algebra in
{\mathfrak{H}}
is a C*-algebra.
So for any morphism
\tau
in
{\mathfrak{H}}
(which is a 2-morphism in
{\mathcal{A}}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{B}}
),
\tau^{*}\circ\tau
is contained in such an endomorphism C*-algebra
{\mathcal{E}}
.
So there exists a positive
\sigma\in{\mathcal{E}}
such that
\sigma^{4}=\tau^{*}\circ\tau\circ\tau^{*}\circ\tau
.
We claim that
\sigma^{2}=\tau^{*}\circ\tau
.
Let
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}
be a
{\dagger}
-2-functor, which we may extend to a representation of
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
by continuity.
The fact that
\sigma\geq 0
implies that
F(\sigma^{2})\geq 0
.
Now notice
F(\sigma^{2})^{2}=F(\tau^{*}\tau)^{2}
.
Since
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}
is a C*-2-category,
F(\tau^{*}\tau)=F(\tau)^{*}F(\tau)\geq 0
.
Since
F{\mathcal{E}}
is a C*-algebra, the uniqueness of positive square roots yields
F(\sigma^{2})=F(\tau^{*}\tau)
.
Since
F
was arbitrary, we conclude
\sigma^{2}=\tau^{*}\tau
.
Therefore
{\mathfrak{H}}
is a C*-category and we conclude that
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
is a C*-2-category.
∎

Universal Property 2.20
.

For strict C*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, there is a natural cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor

{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}.

For any cubical functor
F:{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
into a strict C*-2-category
{\mathcal{B}}
, there exists a unique strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

Verification.

We provide the data of the cubical functor
C:{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
using Proposition
2.14
. For objects
A_{1}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
A_{2}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, we define the strict
{\dagger}
-2-functors

\displaystyle C_{A_{2}}

\displaystyle\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}

\displaystyle C_{A_{1}}

\displaystyle\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}

to be
-\boxtimes A_{2}
and
A_{1}\boxtimes-
as in Remark
2.18
.
For a pair 1-morphisms
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
respectively, the unitary 2-morphism
\Sigma_{X_{1},X_{2}}
in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
serves the role of
\Sigma_{X_{1},X_{2}}
in Proposition
2.14
. This data satisfies all of the desired axioms by construction of the algebraic Gray tensor product. So we have successfully defined
C\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
.

Let
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
be a cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor, with data of
F
as in Proposition
2.14
. In what follows, we denote the interchanger of
F
by
\Sigma^{F}
. We first determine a strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
:

(0)

on objects,

F(A_{1},A_{2})\coloneqq F_{A_{1}}(A_{2})=F_{A_{2}}(A_{1});

(1)

on generator 1-morphisms,

\displaystyle F(X,\mathrm{id}_{A_{2}})

\displaystyle\coloneqq F_{A_{2}}(X),

\displaystyle F(\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}},Y)

\displaystyle\coloneqq F_{A_{1}}(Y);

(2)

on generator 2-morphisms by

\displaystyle F(x\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{A_{2}}})

\displaystyle\coloneqq F_{A_{2}}(x)

\displaystyle F(\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}}}\otimes y)

\displaystyle\coloneqq F_{A_{1}}(y)

\displaystyle F(\Sigma_{X,Y})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Sigma^{F}_{X,Y}.

We extend
F
to all of
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
as a strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor, which is well-defined by construction.

Now consider some arbitrary 2-morphism
w
in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
. Let
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}\colon{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}
be the universal representation on
{\mathcal{B}}
, which is faithful on all levels. Notice that
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}
is isometric on 2-morphisms by the C*-identity and the fact that injective maps between (endomorphism) C*-algebras are isometric. Since
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}\circ F\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}
is a
{\dagger}
-2-functor, we obtain

\|F(w)\|=\|(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}\circ F)(w)\|\leq\|w\|_{\mu}.

Therefore, we may uniquely extend
F
to
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
by continuity.
∎

Proposition 2.21

(Unitary hom-tensor Adjunction)
.

For strict C*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, and
{\mathcal{B}}
, we have that the following strict C*-2-categories are unitarily naturally equivalent:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\mathsf{st}}({\mathbb{C}}\to{\mathcal{B}})

\displaystyle\cong{\mathcal{B}},

\displaystyle{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\mathsf{st}}({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}})

\displaystyle\cong{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\mathsf{st}}({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\mathsf{st}}({\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}})).

Proof.

We will merely sketch the assignments at the level of objects for both isomorphisms.
For the first isomorphism, note that we are viewing
{\mathbb{C}}=B^{2}{\mathbb{C}}
as a C*-2-category with one object
\bullet
, a single 1-morphism
\mathrm{id}_{\bullet}
, with

{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{id}_{\bullet}\tto\mathrm{id}_{\bullet})\coloneqq{\mathbb{C}}.

Composition is given by multiplication and
{\dagger}
is given by conjugation.
From this it is easy to see that the assignment

(F\colon{\mathbb{C}}\to{\mathcal{B}})\mapsto F(\bullet)

extends to a strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor which is bijective on all levels.
For the latter isomorphism, one uses Proposition
2.14
to produce a bijective correspondence

(F\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}})\mapsto(A_{1}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\mapsto(F_{A_{1}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}})).

Here we have omitted the assignment of 1-morphisms and 2-morphisms. We do however note that the interchanger
\Sigma
is used to form part of the naturator unitary for the
{\dagger}
-2-natural transformation corresponding to a 1-morphism
X_{1}
in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
. The naturality axiom for this
{\dagger}
-2-natural transformation follows by
(\Sigma 1)
and
(\Sigma 3)
, while the strictness of this
{\dagger}
-2-functor follows by cubicality and
(\Sigma 2)
. For 2-morphisms in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
, the obviously assigned uniformly bounded modification in
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\mathsf{st}}({\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}})
satisfies the modification axiom by
(\Sigma 1)
as well.
∎

We obtain the following result as a corollary of
[
EK66
, §II.3,4]
.

Corollary 2.22
.

{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}\coloneqq({\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\mathsf{st}},\maxboxtimes)
forms a closed symmetric monoidal category.

2.5
W*-completion of a C*-2-category

For a C*-2-category
{\mathcal{A}}
, we wish to construct the enveloping W*-2-category
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}({\mathcal{A}})
together with a monic
{\dagger}
-2-functor
{\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{W}}^{*}({\mathcal{A}})
, which satisfies the following universal property:

•

For every
{\dagger}
-2-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
into a W*-2-category
{\mathcal{B}}
, there exists a unique normal extension making the following diagram commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{W}}^{*}({\mathcal{A}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!\widetilde{F}}

Consider the C*-category enriched graph
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
with vertices
\Ob{\mathcal{A}}
and edges
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(A\to B)\coloneqq{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)^{**}
for each pair
A,B\in{\mathcal{A}}
. Here
{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)^{**}
is the enveloping W*-category described in Appendix
A.3
. We define two Arens 1-compositions on
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
, which serve to equip
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
with the structure of a
{\dagger}
-2-category.

Definition 2.23
.

For 1-morphisms in
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
, we define the left and right Arens 1-compositions
\ccirc_{\ell}
and
\ccirc_{r}
to act as 1-composition
\ccirc
on
{\mathcal{A}}
.

For 1-composable 2-morphisms
\Phi\in{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(\mbox{}_{A}{X}_{B}\tto\mbox{}_{A}{Y}_{B})
and
\Psi\in{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(\mbox{}_{B}{X^{\prime}\!}_{C}\tto\mbox{}_{B}{Y^{\prime}\!}_{C})
, we
define

\Phi\ccirc_{\ell}\Psi,\,\Phi\ccirc_{r}\Psi\in{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(\mbox{}_{A}{X\ccirc_{B}X^{\prime}\!}_{C}\tto\mbox{}_{A}{Y\ccirc_{B}Y^{\prime}\!}_{C})

(
\ell
)

For
\varphi\in{\mathcal{A}}(\mbox{}_{A}{X\ccirc X^{\prime}\!}_{C}\tto\mbox{}_{A}{Y\ccirc Y^{\prime}\!}_{C})^{*}
, we set
(\Phi\ccirc_{\ell}\Psi)(\varphi)\coloneqq\Phi(\varphi\triangleleft\Psi)
where

\varphi\triangleleft\Psi\in{\mathcal{A}}(\mbox{}_{A}{X}_{B}\tto\mbox{}_{A}{Y}_{B})^{*}

is given by:

(
\triangleleft
)

For
a\in{\mathcal{A}}(\mbox{}_{A}{X}_{B}\tto\mbox{}_{A}{Y}_{B})
, we set
(\varphi\triangleleft\Psi)(a)\coloneqq\Psi(a\triangleright\varphi)
where

a\triangleright\varphi\in{\mathcal{A}}(\mbox{}_{B}{X^{\prime}\!}_{C}\tto\mbox{}_{B}{Y^{\prime}\!}_{C})^{*}

is given by:

(
\triangleright
)

For
b\in{\mathcal{A}}(\mbox{}_{B}{X^{\prime}\!}_{C}\tto\mbox{}_{B}{Y^{\prime}\!}_{C})
, we set
(a\triangleright\varphi)(b)\coloneqq\varphi(a\ccirc b)
.

More succinctly,
\Phi\ccirc_{\ell}\Psi
is given by the following formula:

\displaystyle\Phi\ccirc_{\ell}\Psi

\displaystyle=\varphi\mapsto\Phi\Big(\varphi\triangleleft\Psi\Big),

\displaystyle=\varphi\mapsto\Phi\Big(a\mapsto\Psi\big(a\triangleright\varphi\big)\Big),

\displaystyle=\varphi\mapsto\Phi\Big(a\mapsto\Psi\big(b\mapsto\varphi(a\ccirc b)\big)\Big).

(
r
)

For
\varphi\in{\mathcal{A}}(\mbox{}_{A}{X\ccirc X^{\prime}\!}_{C}\tto\mbox{}_{A}{Y\ccirc Y^{\prime}\!}_{C})^{*}
, we set
(\Phi\ccirc_{r}\Psi)(\varphi)\coloneqq\Psi(\Phi\triangleright\varphi)
where

\Phi\triangleright\varphi\in{\mathcal{A}}(\mbox{}_{B}{X^{\prime}\!}_{C}\tto\mbox{}_{B}{Y^{\prime}\!}_{C})^{*}

is given by:

(
\triangleright
)

For
b\in{\mathcal{A}}(\mbox{}_{B}{X^{\prime}\!}_{C}\tto\mbox{}_{B}{Y^{\prime}\!}_{C})
, we set
(\Phi\triangleright\varphi)(b)\coloneqq\Phi(\varphi\triangleleft b)
where

\varphi\triangleleft b\in{\mathcal{A}}(\mbox{}_{A}{X}_{B}\tto\mbox{}_{A}{Y}_{B})^{*}

is given by:

(
\triangleleft
)

For
a\in{\mathcal{A}}(\mbox{}_{A}{X}_{B}\tto\mbox{}_{A}{Y}_{B})
, we set
(\varphi\triangleleft b)(a)\coloneqq\varphi(a\ccirc b)
.

More succinctly,
\Phi\ccirc_{r}\Psi
is given by the following formula:

\displaystyle\Phi\ccirc_{r}\Psi

\displaystyle=\varphi\mapsto\Psi\Big(\Phi\triangleright\varphi\Big),

\displaystyle=\varphi\mapsto\Psi\Big(b\mapsto\Phi\big(\varphi\triangleleft b\big)\Big),

\displaystyle=\varphi\mapsto\Psi\Big(b\mapsto\Phi\big(a\mapsto\varphi(a\ccirc b)\big)\Big).

Proposition 2.24
.

For a C*-2-category
{\mathcal{A}}
, the left and right Arens 1-compositions on
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
coincide and serve to equip
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
with the structure of a W*-2-category.

Proof.

Notice the following facts about
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}_{\ell}\coloneqq({\mathcal{A}}^{**},\ccirc_{\ell})
and
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}_{r}\coloneqq({\mathcal{A}}^{**},\ccirc_{r})
.

•

\ev_{a}\ccirc_{\ell}\ev_{b}=\ev_{a\ccirc b}=\ev_{a}\ccirc_{r}\ev_{b}
for 1-composable 2-morphisms
a,b
in
{\mathcal{A}}
.
Therefore, we may upgrade
\ev
into
{\dagger}
-2-functors
{\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{A}}^{**}_{\ell}
and
{\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{A}}^{**}_{r}
which act as the identity on objects, and as the
{\dagger}
-functor
\ev
on hom W*-categories.

•

{\mathcal{A}}^{**}_{\ell}
and
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}_{r}
inherit associators
\ev_{\alpha_{XYZ}}
which are natural since
\operatorname{Im}\ev
is dense in
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
and 2-composition is weak*-continuous in each hom W*-category of
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
.
Similarly, units
\mathrm{id}_{A}
and unitors
\ev_{\lambda_{X}}
,
\ev_{\rho_{X}}
are inherited from
{\mathcal{A}}
.

Therefore
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}_{\ell}
and
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}_{r}
are C*-2-categories.
It remains to show that
\ccirc_{\ell}
and
\ccirc_{r}
are separately normal.
It is clear that
-\ccirc_{\ell}\Psi
and
\Phi\ccirc_{r}-
are normal for 2-morphisms
\Phi,\Psi
in
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
.

We will now show condition
(W*2
′
)
, that is
-\ccirc_{\ell}\ev_{\mathrm{id}_{X^{\prime}}}
and
\ev_{\mathrm{id}_{X}}\ccirc_{\ell}-
are separately normal for 1-morphisms
X,X^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
.
For
\Phi_{\lambda}\to\Phi
in the weak topology, we have

(\Phi_{\lambda}\ccirc_{\ell}\ev_{\mathrm{id}_{X^{\prime}}})(\varphi)=\Phi_{\lambda}(\varphi\triangleleft\ev_{\mathrm{id}_{X^{\prime}}})\to\Phi(\varphi\triangleleft\ev_{\mathrm{id}_{X^{\prime}}})=(\Phi\ccirc_{\ell}\ev_{\mathrm{id}_{X^{\prime}}})(\varphi).

Hence
\Phi_{\lambda}\ccirc_{\ell}\ev_{\mathrm{id}_{X^{\prime}}}\to\Phi\ccirc_{\ell}\ev_{\mathrm{id}_{X^{\prime}}}
weakly.
Moreover, for
\Psi_{\lambda}\to\Psi
in the weak topology, observe

\displaystyle(\ev_{\mathrm{id}_{X}}\ccirc\Psi_{\lambda})(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\ev_{\mathrm{id}_{X}}(\varphi\triangleleft\Psi_{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\Psi_{\lambda}(b\mapsto\varphi(\mathrm{id}_{X}\ccirc b))

\displaystyle\to\Psi(b\mapsto\varphi(\mathrm{id}_{X}\ccirc b))

\displaystyle=(\ev_{\mathrm{id}_{X}}\ccirc\Psi_{\lambda})(\varphi).

Hence
\ev_{\mathrm{id}_{X}}\ccirc\Psi_{\lambda}\to\ev_{id_{X}}\ccirc\Psi
weakly. Therefore
\ccirc_{\ell}
is separately normal, and one similarly shows
\ccirc_{r}
is separately normal. Finally, since
\operatorname{Im}\ev
is dense at the level of two morphisms,
\ccirc_{\ell}
and
\ccirc_{r}
are separately normal, and they agree on
\operatorname{Im}\ev
, we conclude that
\ccirc_{\ell}=\ccirc_{r}
on
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
.
∎

Universal Property 2.25
.

For every
{\dagger}
-2-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
into a W*-2-category
{\mathcal{B}}
, there exists a unique normal
{\dagger}
-2-functor
\widetilde{F}\colon{\mathcal{A}}^{**}\to{\mathcal{B}}
making the following diagram commute.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}^{**}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!\widetilde{F}}

Verification.

First note that
\widetilde{F}
must acts on objects as
F
. Then, for every hom C*-category
{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
, we may extend
F
to
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(A\to B)
by the universal property of W*-envelopes or C*-categories. This yields a map
\widetilde{F}:{\mathcal{A}}^{**}\to{\mathcal{B}}^{**}
which is locally a normal
{\dagger}
-functor. Then observe that the tensorator and unitor of
F
equips
\widetilde{F}
with the structure of a
{\dagger}
-2-functor since
\ccirc
and
\circ
are separately weak*-continuous and
\operatorname{Im}\ev
is weak*-dense in
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
.
∎

As a corollary, we obtain another proof of the concreteness theorem for C*-2-categories.

Theorem 2.26

(Gelfand-Naimark for C*-2-categories)
.

For a small C*-2-category
{\mathcal{A}}
, the universal representation

\Upsilon_{2}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathsf{Hilb^{W^{*}}}

given by
{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{\ev}{\mathcal{A}}^{**}\xrightarrow{\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}}{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}\xrightarrow{\GNS}\mathsf{Hilb^{W^{*}}}

is monic. Thus, every C*-2-category
{\mathcal{A}}
can be realized as a norm-closed strict
{\dagger}
-2-category
\GNS_{2}({\mathcal{A}})\coloneqq\operatorname{Im}\Upsilon_{2}
of W*-categories of Hilbert spaces and operators. Moreover, if
{\mathcal{A}}
is strict,
\Upsilon_{2}
is a strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor.

Remark 2.27
.

We expect a Sherman-​Takeda theorem to hold for C*-2-categories.
Namely, for a small C*-2-category
{\mathcal{A}}
, the
{\dagger}
-2-functor

\widetilde{\Upsilon}_{2}\colon{\mathcal{A}}^{**}\to\GNS_{2}({\mathcal{A}})^{\prime\prime}

given by the universal property of the W*-envelope of a C*-2-category is an equivalence of W*-2-categories extending
\Upsilon_{2}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to\GNS_{2}({\mathcal{A}})
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\GNS_{2}({\mathcal{A}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}^{**}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\GNS_{2}({\mathcal{A}})^{\prime\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{\Upsilon}}_{2}}

2.6
W*-Gray tensor product

For strict W*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, we wish to construct a W*-2-category
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
together with a separately normal cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
satisfying the following universal property:

•

For every separately normal cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor
H\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
into a strict W*-2-category
{\mathcal{B}}
, there exists a unique normal
{\dagger}
-2-functor
H\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
such that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}

Definition 2.28
.

For strict W*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, and composible 1-morphisms
X_{i},Y_{i}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
for
i=1,2
, we define

({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})\big((X_{1},X_{2})\to(Y_{1},Y_{2})\big)_{*}\subset({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})\big((X_{1},X_{2})\to(X_{1},X_{2})\big)^{*}

as follows. First, let
\SN\big((X_{1},X_{2})\to(Y_{1},Y_{2})\big)
be the closed subspace consisting of all functionals which are separately normal, i.e. the functionals
\varphi\in({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})\big((X_{1},X_{2})\to(Y_{1},Y_{2})\big)^{*}
such that

\displaystyle\varphi\circ(\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}}}\otimes-)\in{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(X_{2}\to Y_{2})_{*},\quad

\displaystyle\text{for every }A_{1}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{1},\text{ and}

\displaystyle\varphi\circ(-\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{A_{2}}})\in{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(X_{1}\to X_{1})_{*},\quad

\displaystyle\text{for every }A_{2}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{2}.

We then define
({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})_{*}
to be the largest closed subspace of
\SN
invariant under the four actions of
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
on
({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})^{*}
given by precomposing and postcomposing the argument of
\varphi\in\SN
horizontally (1-composition
\otimes
) and vertically (2-composition
\circ
).

Remark 2.29
.

We are using the suggestive notation
({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})\big((X_{1},X_{2})\to(Y_{1},Y_{2})\big)_{*}
even though we have not yet defined the W*-category
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
. However, once we do construct this W*-Gray tensor product, we will see that this Banach space is indeed the predual of the hom space
({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})\big((X_{1},X_{2})\to(Y_{1},Y_{2})\big)
.

The following result will serve as motivation for the definition of the W*-Gray tensor product
\Wboxtimes
.

Proposition 2.30
.

For objects
X,Y\in{\mathcal{B}}
in a strict W*-2-category
{\mathcal{B}}
, consider the polar

{\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Y)_{*}^{\perp}\coloneqq\left\{\Phi\in{\mathcal{B}}^{**}(X\tto Y)\,\middle|\,\Phi(\varphi)=0\text{ for all }\varphi\in{\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Y)_{*}\right\}.

Then
{\mathcal{B}}_{*}^{\perp}\subseteq{\mathcal{B}}^{**}
is closed under pre- and post- 1-composition and 2-composition of 2-morphisms in
{\mathcal{B}}^{**}
, so that
{\mathcal{B}}^{**}/{\mathcal{B}}_{*}^{\perp}
is a (strict) W*-2-category.
Moreover, if
\pi\colon{\mathcal{B}}^{**}\to{\mathcal{B}}^{**}/{\mathcal{B}}^{\perp}_{*}
is the natural quotient
{\dagger}
-2-functor, then the composite

{\mathcal{B}}\xhookrightarrow{\ev}{\mathcal{B}}^{**}\xrightarrow{\pi}{\mathcal{B}}^{**}/{\mathcal{B}}^{\perp}_{*}

is an equivalence of (strict) W*-2-categories.

Proof.

Let us first show that
{\mathcal{B}}_{*}^{\perp}
is closed under both pre-compositions and post-compositions with 2-morphisms in
{\mathcal{B}}^{**}
. First note that normal functionals on hom spaces of
{\mathcal{B}}
are closed under the four actions of
{\mathcal{B}}
since 1-composition and 2-composition of 2-morphisms are separately normal in a W*-2-category. One can then use the fact that left and right Arens 1-compositions and 2-compositions agree respectively to show that
{\mathcal{B}}^{\perp}_{*}
is closed under pre- and post- 1-composing and 2-composing 2-morphisms in
{\mathcal{B}}^{**}
. Indeed, if
\Phi\in{\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Y)_{*}^{\perp}
and
\Psi\in{\mathcal{B}}^{**}(Y\tto Z)
for parallel 1-morphisms
X,Y,Z
in
{\mathcal{B}}
, there exists some
\ev_{x_{\lambda}}\to\Psi
weak* in
{\mathcal{B}}^{**}
. So for
\varphi\in{\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Z)_{*}
we have
\{\varphi\triangleleft x_{\lambda}\}\subset{\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Y)_{*}
and hence

(\Psi\circ\Phi)(\varphi)=\lim_{\lambda}(\ev_{x_{\lambda}}\circ_{r}\Phi)(\varphi)=\lim_{\lambda}\Phi(\varphi\triangleleft x_{\lambda})=0.

Therefore
\Psi\circ\Phi\in{\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Z)_{*}^{\perp}
, and one similarly shows our three other claims.
Therefore
{\mathcal{B}}^{**}/{\mathcal{B}}^{\perp}_{*}
is a C*-2-category. For Banach spaces
E\subseteq F
, it is an elementary fact that

F^{*}/E^{\perp}\cong E^{*}

via the map
[\Phi]\to\Phi|_{E}
for
\Phi\in F^{*}
.
In particular, we have

({\mathcal{B}}^{**}/{\mathcal{B}}^{\perp}_{*})(X\tto Y)_{*}={\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Y)_{*},

i.e.
({\mathcal{B}}^{**}/{\mathcal{B}}_{*}^{\perp})(X\tto Y)\cong{\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Y)_{*}^{*}\cong{\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Y)
. We then conclude
{\mathcal{B}}^{**}/{\mathcal{B}}^{\perp}_{*}
is a W*-2-category.

It is now easy to see that
\pi\circ\ev\colon{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{B}}^{**}/{\mathcal{B}}_{*}^{\perp}
is an isomorphism of W*-2-categories.
Indeed, when viewing
{\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Y)_{*}\subseteq{\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Y)^{*}
as the subspace of normal functionals on
{\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Y)
, the isomorphism

{\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Y)\to{\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Y)_{*}^{*}

is given by
x\mapsto\ev_{x}|_{{\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Y)_{*}}
.
On the other hand, the isomorphism

({\mathcal{B}}^{**}/{\mathcal{B}}_{*}^{\perp})(X\tto Y)\to{\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Y)_{*}^{*}

is given by
[\Phi]\to\Phi|_{{\mathcal{B}}(X\tto Y)_{*}}
.
Hence, the map
\pi\circ\ev
given by
x\mapsto[\ev_{x}]
is an isomorphism on each hom-space. Since both
\pi
and
\ev
act as the identity on objects and 1-morphisms, we conclude that
\pi\circ\ev
is an (automatically normal) equivalence of W*-2-categories.∎

Definition 2.31
.

For strict W*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}_{1},{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, we define their W*-Gray tensor product to be

{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\coloneqq({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})^{**}/({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})_{*}^{\perp}.

Lemma 2.32
.

The W*-Gray tensor product
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
is a (strict) W*-2-category.

Proof.

Since
({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})_{*}^{\perp}\subseteq({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})^{**}
is closed under pre- and post- 1-composition and 2-composition of 2-morphisms in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wmaxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
by definition, it follows that
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wmaxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
is indeed a well-defined C*-2-category. As before, it is an elementary fact that the predual of each hom-space is the previously-defined
({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})_{*}
. Hence
\ccirc
is separately normal on
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
as it is separately normal on
({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})^{**}
, and we conclude that
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
is indeed a W*-2-category.
∎

For the remaining portion of this section, we shall fix W*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}_{1},{\mathcal{A}}_{2},{\mathcal{B}}
and a separately normal cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor
H\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
.

Lemma 2.33
.

Consider the induced
{\dagger}
-2-functor
\widetilde{H}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
on the C*-Gray tensor product. For parallel 1-morphisms
X_{i},Y_{i}
in
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
where
i=1,2
and
\psi\in{\mathcal{B}}\big(\widetilde{H}(X_{1},X_{2})\to\widetilde{H}(Y_{1},Y_{2})\big)_{*}
, we may define

\widetilde{H}^{*}\psi\in({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})\big((X_{1},X_{2})\to(Y_{1},Y_{2})\big)^{*}\quad\text{by }(\widetilde{H}^{*}\psi)(t)\coloneqq\psi(\widetilde{H}t).

Then
\widetilde{H}^{*}\psi\in({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})\big((X_{1},X_{2})\to(X_{1},X_{2})\big)_{*}
. We will denote this by

\widetilde{H}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{*}\subseteq({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})_{*}

for simplicity.

Proof.

Let
A_{1}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and suppose
a_{2}^{\lambda}\to a_{2}
weak* in
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(A_{2}\to B_{2})
. Since
H
is separately normal,
H(\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}}}\otimes a_{2}^{\lambda})\to H(\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}}}\otimes a_{2})

\sigma
-WOT. Since
\psi
is normal, we conclude

(\widetilde{H}^{*}\psi)(\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}}}\otimes a_{2}^{\lambda})=\psi(H(\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}}}\otimes a_{2}^{\lambda}))\to\psi(H(\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}}}\otimes a_{2}))=(\widetilde{H}^{*}\psi)(\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}}}\otimes a_{2}).

A similar argument reveals
(\widetilde{H}^{*}\psi)(a^{\lambda}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{A_{2}}})\to(\widetilde{H}^{*}\psi)(a_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}}})
when
a_{1}^{\lambda}\to a_{1}
weak* in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
. Therefore
\widetilde{H}^{*}\psi
is a separately normal functional. Moreover, since
H
is a
{\dagger}
-2-functor and both 1-composition and 2-composition of 2-morphisms in
{\mathcal{B}}
is separately normal, it is easy to see that
\widetilde{H}^{*}\psi\in({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})_{*}
.
∎

Note that the cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
given by

{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\xrightarrow{\ev}({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})^{**}\xrightarrow{\pi}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}

is separately normal since
({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})_{*}
consists of functionals on
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
which are separately normal.

Universal Property 2.34
.

For strict W*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, if
H\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
is a separately normal cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor into a strict W*-2-category
{\mathcal{B}}
, then there exists a unique normal
{\dagger}
-2-functor
\overline{H}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
such that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{H}}

Proof.

By Lemma
2.33
,
\widetilde{H}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{*}\subseteq({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})_{*}
, so
({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})_{*}^{\perp}\subseteq(\widetilde{H}^{*}{\mathcal{B}})_{*}^{\perp}
and
\widetilde{H}^{**}({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})_{*}^{\perp}\subseteq{\mathcal{B}}_{*}^{\perp}
. We then obtain a morphism of short exact sequences:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})_{*}^{\perp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})^{**}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{B}}_{*}^{\perp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{B}}^{**}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{B}}^{**}/{\mathcal{B}}_{*}^{\perp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{**}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\widetilde{H}^{**}]}

We now define the normal functor
{\dagger}
-2-functor
\overline{H}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
to be

\overline{H}\coloneqq{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\xrightarrow{[\widetilde{H}^{**}]}{\mathcal{B}}/{\mathcal{B}}^{\perp}\xrightarrow{(\pi\circ\ev)^{-1}}{\mathcal{B}}.

The desired triangle commutes by the commutativity of the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})^{**}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{B}}^{**}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{B}}/{\mathcal{B}}_{*}^{\perp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{**}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\widetilde{H}^{**}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}

To see that
\overline{H}
is unique, suppose
K\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
is a normal
{\dagger}
-2-functor making the following diagram commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}

By the universal property of the C*-Gray tensor product, we have

\widetilde{H}={\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\xrightarrow{\ev}({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})^{**}\xrightarrow{\pi}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\xrightarrow{K}{\mathcal{B}}.

We also know
\ev\circ\widetilde{H}=\widetilde{H}^{**}\circ\ev
, so by the universal property of W*-completion, we have

\widetilde{H}^{**}=({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})^{**}\xrightarrow{\pi}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\xrightarrow{K}{\mathcal{B}}\xrightarrow{\ev}{\mathcal{B}}^{**}.

Finally, we know
\pi\circ\widetilde{H}^{**}=[\widetilde{H}^{**}]\circ\pi
, so the surjectivity of
\pi
implies

[\widetilde{H}^{**}]={\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\xrightarrow{K}{\mathcal{B}}\xrightarrow{\ev}{\mathcal{B}}^{**}\xrightarrow{\pi}{\mathcal{B}}^{**}/{\mathcal{B}}_{*}^{\perp}.

From this we conclude that
K=\overline{H}
and hence
\overline{H}
is unique.
∎

Definition 2.35
.

Let
F_{i}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{i}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\prime}
be normal
{\dagger}
-2-functors between strict W*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
for
i=1,2
. We define
F_{1}\Wboxtimes F_{2}
to be the normal
{\dagger}
-2-functor induced by the universal property of
\Wboxtimes
as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{\prime}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{\prime}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}\maxblacktimes F_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}\Wmaxtimes F_{2}}

Remark 2.36
.

Due to the uniqueness of normal
{\dagger}
-2-functors induced by the universal property of
\Wboxtimes
, it is easy to see that
\Wboxtimes\colon{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\mathsf{st}}\times{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\mathsf{st}}\to{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\mathsf{st}}
is a functor when viewing
\Wboxtimes
as a 1-category of strict W*-2-categories and normal
{\dagger}
-2-functors.

Theorem 2.37

(Unitary hom-Tensor Adjunction)
.

For strict W*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, and
{\mathcal{B}}
, we have that the following W*-2-categories are unitarily naturally isomorphic:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\mathsf{st}}({\mathbb{C}}\to{\mathcal{B}})

\displaystyle\cong{\mathcal{B}},

\displaystyle{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\mathsf{st}}({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wboxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}})

\displaystyle\cong{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\mathsf{st}}({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\to{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\mathsf{st}}({\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}})).

Proof.

This follows from an identical proof to that of Proposition
2.21
, using the fact that
\Wboxtimes
represents separately normal cubical functors to obtain normal strict
{\dagger}
-2-functors as expected.
∎

As in the C* case, we obtain the following as a corollary of
[
EK66
, §II.3,4]
.

Corollary 2.38
.

{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}\coloneqq({\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\mathsf{st}},\Wboxtimes)
forms a closed symmetric monoidal category.

2.7
Cofibrant replacement for operator 2-categories

In the following section, we provide cofibrant replacement results for operator 2-categories, (normal)
{\dagger}
-functors, as well as a tensorator for this replacement. This section follows the treatment in
[
Gur13
, §2.2]
closely, so we have provided the details in Appendix §
B.2
.

Proposition 2.39
.

For every C*-2-category
{\mathcal{A}}
, there exists a strict C*-2-category
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}
together with an epic 2-equivalence
\ev_{{\mathcal{A}}}\colon\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
. Moreover, when
{\mathcal{A}}
is a W*-2-category we have that
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}
is a W*-2-category and hence
\ev_{{\mathcal{A}}}
is automatically normal.

Proposition 2.40
.

For each
{\dagger}
-2-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
between C*-2-categories, there exists a strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor
\widehat{F}\colon\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{B}}}
and a unitary icon
3
3

3

By an icon, we mean a strictified version of a 2-natural transformation between 2-functors that agree on objects
[
Lac08
]
.

u^{F}
as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{{\mathcal{B}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{{\mathcal{B}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{F}}

Moreover, when
F
is a normal
{\dagger}
-2-functor between W*-2-categories, then
\widehat{F}
is also normal.

Proposition 2.41
.

For C*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, there exists a cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor

C\colon\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}\maxblacktimes\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{2}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}

which is the identity on objects, and a unitary icon
u
as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}\maxblacktimes\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\ev}}

Moreover, when

\overline{C}\colon\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}\Wblacktimes\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{2}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}

and

to a unitary icon
\overline{u}
as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}\Wblacktimes\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{C}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{u}^{\ev}}

3
3-categorical results

3.1
Operator 3-categories

Definition 3.1
.

A C*-3-category
{\mathcal{A}}
consists of an algebraic tricategory in the sense of
[
Gur13
]
, equipped with a C*-2-category structure on each hom 2-category
{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
, such that the underlying coherence 2-functors, 2-natural transformations, and modifications are
{\dagger}
-2-functors,
{\dagger}
-2-natural transformations, and unitary modifications respectively.

Remark 3.2
.

We unpack the data of a C*-3-category as follows:

(0)

A collection
\Ob T
of
objects
, or
0-cells
, of
{\mathcal{A}}
;

(hom)

For
A,B\in\Ob T
, a C*-2-category
{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
where:

•

The objects of
{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
are called
1-cells
of
{\mathcal{A}}
with source
A
and target
B
,

•

The arrows of
{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
will be referred to as
2-cells
of
{\mathcal{A}}
with their same source and target, and

•

The 2-morphisms of
{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
are called
3-cells
of
{\mathcal{A}}
, also with their same source and target.

As
[
Gur13
]
, we will denote
{\mathcal{A}}(A_{0}\to A_{1})\maxblacktimes\cdots\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}(A_{n-1}\to A_{n})
by the abbreviation

{\mathcal{A}}^{n}(A_{0}\to\cdots\to A_{n}).

(
\cccirc
)

For
A,B,C\in\Ob{\mathcal{A}}
, a
{\dagger}
-2-functor
\cccirc\colon{\mathcal{A}}^{2}(A\to B\to C)\to{\mathcal{A}}(A\to C)
called
1-composition
. In the graphical calculus for the monoidal 3-category
({\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat},\maxtimes)
, we represent
\cccirc
by:

{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
{\mathcal{A}}(B\to C)
{\mathcal{A}}(A\to C)

(I)

For
A\in\Ob{\mathcal{A}}
, a
{\dagger}
-2-functor
I_{A}\colon{\mathbb{C}}\to{\mathcal{A}}(A\to A)
which we represent diagrammatically by:

{\mathcal{A}}(A\to A)

(a)

For
A,B,C,D\in\Ob{\mathcal{A}}
, an
“associator”
unitary adjoint equivalence
\mathbf{a}=(a,a^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}},\epsilon^{a},\eta^{a})
where
a
is a

{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}({\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)\to{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B))
;

(
\pi
)

For
A,B,C,D,E\in\Ob{\mathcal{A}}
, a
“pentagonator”
unitary modification

\pi\atop\ttto

\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}

in
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}({\mathcal{A}}^{4}(A\to B\to C\to D\to E)\to{\mathcal{A}}(A\to E)).

(coh)

For
A,B,C\in\Ob{\mathcal{A}}
,
middle, left, and right unity coheretor
unitary modification

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rotatebox{135.0}{$\ttto$}\mu}

\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\raisebox{7.0pt}{\rotatebox{270.0}{$\ttto$}}\,\lambda}

\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\raisebox{5.0pt}{\rotatebox{270.0}{$\ttto$}}\,\rho}

\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}}

in
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}({\mathcal{A}}^{2}(A\to B\to C)\to{\mathcal{A}}(A\to C))
.

Furthermore, we ask that data of objects together with underlying hom-2-categories, functors, adjoint equivalences, and invertible modifications form a 3-category (algebraic tricategory) in the sense of
[
Gur13
, Definition 4.1]
.
More explicitly, these include a 3-dimensional associahedron axiom for each tuple of 5 objects in
{\mathcal{A}}
(non-abelian 4-cocycle condition) and two axioms for each triplet of objects in
{\mathcal{A}}
relating the unity coheretors with the associator and pentagonators (normalized cocycle conditions).

Definition 3.3
.

A W*-3-category
{\mathcal{A}}
is a C*-3-category such that:

•

Each
{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
is a W*-2-category;

•

The 1-composition
{\dagger}
-2-functor
\cccirc
is separately normal, hence extending to a normal
{\dagger}
-2-functor

\cccirc\colon{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)\Wblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}(B\to C)\to{\mathcal{A}}(A\to C).

Note that the unit
{\dagger}
-2-functor
I_{A}\colon{\mathcal{A}}(A\to A)
is automatically normal. Using the fact that 2-composition
\ccirc
and 3-composition
\circ
are separately normal on each hom-W*-2-category, we may extend the constraint unitary adjoint equivalences so that they all occur in
({\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat},\Wmaxtimes)
.

Definition 3.4
.

A
{\dagger}
-3-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
between C*-3-categories consists of an underlying 3-functor in the sense of
[
Gur13
]
, such that
F
is locally a
{\dagger}
-2-functor and the underlying coherence 2-natural transformations and modifications are
{\dagger}
-natural transformations and unitary modifications respectively.

Remark 3.5
.

We unpack the data of a
{\dagger}
-3-functor as follows:

(0)

A function from objects of
{\mathcal{A}}
to objects of
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
;

(hom)

For
A,B\in{\mathcal{A}}
, a
{\dagger}
-2-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}(A,B)\to{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}(FA,FB)
, which we represent diagrammatically by:

F
{\mathcal{A}}(A,B)
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}(FA,FB)

(
\cccirc
)

A unitary adjoint equivalence
\mathbf{F^{2}}
where
F^{2}
is a
{\dagger}
-2-natural transformation

Definition 3.6
.

A
{\dagger}
-3-natural transformation
\theta\colon F\tto F^{\prime}
between
{\dagger}
-3-functors

consists of an underlying 3-natural transformation in the sense of
[
Gur13
]
such that the underlying coherence 2-natural transformations and modifications are
{\dagger}
-2-natural transformations and unitary modifications respectively.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

(hom)

A unitary modification
m

\theta_{\!\!A^{\prime}}
F
\theta^{\prime}_{\!\!A^{\prime}}
F
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{m\atop\ttto}}
\theta_{\!A}
F^{\prime}
\theta^{\prime}_{\!A}
F^{\prime}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{A^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{A}}

in
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}({\mathcal{A}}(A\to A^{\prime})\to{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}(FA\to F^{\prime}A^{\prime}))
.

Furthermore, we ask that the data of the underlying family of 2-morphisms and invertible modifications form a 3-modification in the sense of
[
Gur13
, Definition 4.18]
. More explicitly, these include a monoidality/composability axiom and a unitality axiom.

Definition 3.10
.

A uniformly bounded perturbation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma\colon m}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{\prime}}

between
{\dagger}
-3-modifications consists of a family of 3-morphisms
\sigma_{A}\in{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}(m_{a}\ttto n_{a})
indexed by objects in
{\mathcal{A}}
, satisfying the obvious compatibility axiom with the higher data for
m
and
n
, and

\|\sigma\|\coloneqq\sup_{A\in{\mathcal{A}}}\|\sigma_{A}\|<\infty.

We refer the curious reader to
[
Gur13
, Definition 4.21]
.

Example 3.11
.

From
[
CP22
]
we see that
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}
forms a C*-3-category. Hence, the sub-
{\dagger}
-3-category
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\Strict}
of strict C*-2-categories, strict
{\dagger}
-2-functors, all
{\dagger}
-2-natural transformations, and uniformly bounded modifications forms a C*-3-category.

Analogously,
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}
forms a W*-3-category and there is a sub-W*-3-category
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\Strict}
of strict W*-2-categories, strict normal
{\dagger}
-2-functors, all
{\dagger}
-2-natural transformations, and uniformly bounded modifications.

Moreover, there are full sub-W*-3-categories of
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}
, denoted by
\mathsf{Hilb^{W^{*}2}}
and
\mathsf{FSSU2C}
, whose objects are concrete W*-2-categories and finitely semisimple unitary 2-categories respectively.

Example 3.12
.

A monoidal C*-2-category can be viewed as a C*-3-category with a single object. Similarly, a braided C*-category can be viewed as a C*-3-category with a single object, and a single (identity) 1-morphism. In particular, every unitary braided multifusion category can be viewed as a W*-3-category.

The following examples are the appropriate operator-algebraic analogues for Morita 2-categories. We refer the reader to
[
CHPJP22
]
for a detailed exposition.

Example 3.13
.

There is a C*-3-category
\CHaus
whose:

(0)

objects are compact Hausdorff spaces;

(1)

1-morphisms
A\colon X\to Y
are C*-algebras
A
equipped with
*
-homomorphisms
C(X)\to Z(A)
and
C(Y)\to Z(A)
;

(2)

2-morphisms
H\colon A\tto B
are
A
-
B
C*-correspondences compatible with the
C(X)
and
C(Y)
actions,

(3)

3-morphisms are adjointable intertwiners.

One defines the analogous W*-3-category
\Meas
whose:

(0)

objects are
\sigma
