-finite measure spaces
(X,\mu
);

(1)

1-morphisms
A\colon(X,\mu)\to(Y,\nu)
are W*-algebras
A
equipped with normal
*
-homomorphisms
L^{\infty}(X,\mu)\to Z(A)
and
L^{\infty}(Y,\nu)\to Z(A)
;

(2)

2-morphisms
H\colon A\tto B
are
A
-
B
W*-correspondences compatible with the
L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
and
L^{\infty}(Y,\nu)
actions;

(3)

3-morphisms are adjointable normal intertwiners.

We verify the details in Appendix §
C
.

In what follows we make reference to multifusion categories and their bimodule categories, functors, and natural transformations. We direct the reader to the standard reference
[
EGNO17
]
for details.

Example 3.14
.

There is a W*-3-category
\mathsf{UmFC}
whose:

(0)

objects are unitary multifusion categories;

(1)

1-morphisms
M\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
are unitary
{\mathcal{C}}
-
{\mathcal{D}}
bimodule categories;

(2)

2-morphisms
F\colon M\tto N
are unitary
{\mathcal{C}}
-
{\mathcal{D}}
bimodule functors;

(3)

3-morphisms
\eta\colon F\ttto G
are
{\mathcal{C}}
-
{\mathcal{D}}
bimodule natural transformations.

Remark 3.15
.

We conjecture there exists an equivalence
\mathsf{Mod}^{\dagger}\colon\mathsf{UmFC}\cong\mathsf{FSSU2C}
, where the latter is the W*-3-category of finitely semisimple unitary 2-categories.

The following example is the appropriate operator-algebraic analogue of a hom 3-category in the speculated Morita 4-category
\mathsf{UBmFC}
of unitary braided multifusion categories.

Example 3.16
.

For unitary braided multifusion categories
{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}
, there is a W*-3-category
\mathsf{UBmFC}({\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}})
whose:

(0)

Objects are unitary multifusion categories
{\mathcal{C}}
equipped with unitary braided functors
{\mathcal{A}}\to Z({\mathcal{C}})\leftarrow{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{rev}}
;

(1)

1-morphisms
M\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
are unitary
{\mathcal{C}}
-
{\mathcal{D}}
bimodule categories together with compatibility data;

(2)

2-morphisms
F\colon M\tto N
are unitary
{\mathcal{C}}
-
{\mathcal{D}}
bimodule functors satisfying compatibility conditions;

(3)

3-morphisms
\eta\colon F\ttto G
are
{\mathcal{C}}
-
{\mathcal{D}}
bimodule natural transformations.

Notice
\mathsf{UBFC}({\mathbb{C}}\to{\mathbb{C}})=\mathsf{UmFC}
.

3.2
Transport and change of structure

In this section, we provide three results which will allow us to locally strictify an operator 3-category, into what will be known as a “cubical” operator 3-category.
In particular, these three results will allow us to strictify hom-2-categories, composition of objects, and units respectively in a compatible way.

Lemma 3.17

(Transport of structure)
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be a C*-3-category and let

\{\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(A,B)\in{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}\}_{A,B\in{\mathcal{A}}}

be a collection of C*-2-categories indexed by pairs of objects in
{\mathcal{A}}
with
{\dagger}
-2-equivalences

F_{A,B}\colon{\mathcal{A}}(A,B)\to\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(A,B)\text{ for every }A,B\in{\mathcal{A}}.

Then we may upgrade this data to a C*-3-category
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
and a
{\dagger}
-3-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
such that

•

\Ob\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}=\Ob{\mathcal{A}}
,

•

\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(A,B)
is the hom-C*-2-category from
A
to
B
in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
,

•

Choosing biadjoint
{\dagger}
-2-equivalences
(F_{AB},F_{AB}^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}},\eta^{F_{AB}},\epsilon^{F_{AB}})
for each
F_{AB}
, 1-composition
\widetilde{\cccirc}
in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
is then defined by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\,2}(A\to B\to C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}^{2}(A\to B\to C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(A\to C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}(A\to C)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{AB}\maxblacktimes F_{BC}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\cccirc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cccirc}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{AC}^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}}

•

F
is a
{\dagger}
-3-equivalence which acts as the identity on objects and as
F_{A,B}
on hom-C*-2-categories.

Moreover, if
{\mathcal{A}}
is a W*-3-category, each
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(A,B)
is a W*-2-category, and each
F_{A,B}
a normal
{\dagger}
-2-equivalence, then
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
is a W*-3-category.

Proof.

The structure equipped on the 3-category
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
and the 3-functor
F
are built using the units and counits of the biadjoint
{\dagger}
-2-equivalences (which are unitaries) and the constraint data on
{\mathcal{A}}
. From this it is quite easy to see that
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
is a C*-3-category and
F
is a
{\dagger}
-3-functor. Furthermore, from the definition of
\widetilde{\cccirc}
we see that 1-composition is separately normal in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
the W*-case, and
F
is automatically normal as a
{\dagger}
-3-equivalence. We refer the reader to
[
Gur13
, §7.4, Theorem 7.22]
for the details of this construction for non-linear 3-categories.
∎

We will state the next two lemmas without proof, as they are similar in spirit to Lemma
3.17
, and refer the interested reader to
[
Gur13
, §7.4, Theorems 7.23, 7.24]
for details.

Lemma 3.18

(Change of composition)
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be a C*-3-category and let

\{\widetilde{\cccirc}\colon{\mathcal{A}}^{2}(A\to B\to C)\to{\mathcal{A}}(A\to C)\}_{A,B,C\in{\mathcal{A}}}

be a collection of
{\dagger}
-2-functors indexed by triples of objects in
{\mathcal{A}}
with
{\dagger}
-2-natural equivalences

F^{2}\colon\cccirc\tto\widetilde{\cccirc}\quad\text{ for every }A,B,C\in{\mathcal{A}}.

Then we may upgrade this data to a C*-3-category
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
and a
{\dagger}
-3-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
such that

•

\Ob\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}=\Ob{\mathcal{A}}
,

•

\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(A\to B)={\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
as C*-2-categories,

•

\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
has the same units
I_{A}
as
{\mathcal{A}}
,

•

1-composition in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
is given by
\widetilde{\cccirc}
,

•

F
is a
{\dagger}
-3-equivalence which as a map acts as the identity, and

•

Choosing unitary adjoint equivalences for
F^{2}
, we obtain the 1-tensorator unitary adjoint equivalences for
F
.

Furthermore, when
{\mathcal{A}}
is a W*-3-category and each
\widetilde{\cccirc}
is separately normal, it is immediate that
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
is a W*-3-category.

Lemma 3.19

(Change of units)
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be a C*-3-category and let

\{\widetilde{I}_{A}\colon{\mathbb{C}}\to{\mathcal{A}}(A,A)\}_{A\in{\mathcal{A}}}

be a collection of
{\dagger}
-2-functors indexed by the objects in
{\mathcal{A}}
with
{\dagger}
-2-natural equivalences

F^{1}\colon I_{A}\tto\widetilde{I}_{A}\quad\text{for every }A\in{\mathcal{A}}.

Then we may upgrade this data to a C*-3-category
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
and a
{\dagger}
-3-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
such that

•

\Ob\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}=\Ob{\mathcal{A}}
,

•

\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(A\to B)={\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
as C*-2-categories,

•

Units in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
are given by
\widetilde{I}_{A}
,

•

\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
has the same 1-composition
\cccirc
as
{\mathcal{A}}
,

•

F
is a
{\dagger}
-3-equivalence which as a map acts as the identity, and

•

Choosing unitary adjoint equivalences for
F^{1}
, we obtain the 1-unitor unitary adjoint equivalences for
F
.

When
{\mathcal{A}}
is a W*-3-category, it is automatic that
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
is a W*-3-category.

3.3
Operator cubical categories

In this section, we provide an intermediate strictification step for our main coherence result.

Definition 3.20
.

We say that a C*-3-category
{\mathcal{A}}
(resp. W*-3-category) if its underlying 3-category is cubical, i.e. for all objects
A,B,C\in{\mathcal{A}}
we have

(hom)

each
{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
is
strict
C*-2-category (resp. W*-2-category);

(
\cccirc
)

composition
\cccirc\colon{\mathcal{A}}^{2}(A\to B\to C)\to{\mathcal{A}}(A\to C)
is a (resp. separately normal)
cubical

{\dagger}
-2-functor;

(I)

the unit
{\dagger}
-2-functor
I_{A}\colon{\mathcal{A}}(A\to A)
is
strict
.

Theorem 3.21
.

Every C*-3-category
{\mathcal{A}}
is equivalent to a cubical C*-3-category
{\mathcal{A}}^{\cubical}
. Furthermore, if
{\mathcal{A}}
is a W*-3-category, then
{\mathcal{A}}^{\cubical}
is a cubical W*-3-category.

Proof.

For a C*-3-category
{\mathcal{A}}
, we define
{\mathcal{A}}^{\cubical}
as follows:

•

\Ob{\mathcal{A}}^{\cubical}=\Ob{\mathcal{A}}
,

•

{\mathcal{A}}^{\cubical}(A\to B)=\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)}
for every
A,B\in{\mathcal{A}}
.

Using
transport of structure
, we obtain a C*-3-category equivalent to
{\mathcal{A}}
with strict hom-C*-2-categories and 1-composition given by

\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)}\maxblacktimes\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}(B\to C)}\xrightarrow{\ev\maxblacktimes\ev}{\mathcal{A}}^{2}(A\to B\to C)\xrightarrow{\cccirc}{\mathcal{A}}(A\to C)\xrightarrow{\ev^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}}\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}(A\to C)},

where we are choosing unitary adjoint equivalences
(\ev,\ev^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}},\eta^{\ev},\epsilon^{\ev})
for
\ev
.
Observe, by Propositions
2.40
and
2.41
we have the following
{\dagger}
-2-natural equivalence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}^{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}\,)^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cccirc}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\cccirc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\cccirc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev\maxblacktimes\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\ev}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\ev}}

between the cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor
\widehat{\cccirc}\circ C
and the previously mentioned 1-composition.
Using
change of composition
, we obtain a C*-3-category with strict hom-C*-2-categories and cubical 1-composition.
Finally, by using
change of units
to replace each
I_{A}
with its strictification, we obtain the desired equivalent cubical C*-3-category
{\mathcal{A}}^{\cubical}
.
In the case when
{\mathcal{A}}
is a W*-3-category, we use the corresponding results to conclude that
{\mathcal{A}}^{\cubical}
is a W*-3-category.
∎

3.4
Coherence and concreteness for operator algebraic tricategories

Definition 3.22
.

A
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}
-category is a category enriched in
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}
in the sense of
[
Kel05
]
.
A functor of
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}
-categories is then just a strict
{\dagger}
-3-functor.
We denote the category of
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}
-categories and strict
{\dagger}
-3-functors by
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{GrayCat}
.

Similarly, a
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}
-category is a category enriched in
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}
.
A functor of
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}
-categories is then just a strict normal
{\dagger}
-3-functor.
We denote the category of
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}
-categories and strict normal
{\dagger}
-3-functors by
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{GrayCat}
.

In what remains, we consider the Yoneda embedding for cubical operator 3-categories, referring the interested reader to Appendix §
D
for the details of this construction.

Proposition 3.23
.

For
{\mathcal{A}}
a C*-3-category and
{\mathcal{B}}
a C*-Gray-category,
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}({\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}})
forms a C*-Gray-category of

(0)

{\dagger}
-3-functors from
{\mathcal{A}}
to
{\mathcal{B}}
,

(\text{hom})

Hom-C*-2-categories
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}(\mbox{}_{{\mathcal{A}}}{F}_{{\mathcal{B}}}\tto\mbox{}_{{\mathcal{A}}}{G}_{{\mathcal{B}}})
as in Appendix §
D
Lemma
D.1
,

(\cccirc)

Cubical 1-composition
\cccirc
as in Appendix §
D
Lemma
D.2

Moreover, when
{\mathcal{A}}
is a W*-3-category and
{\mathcal{B}}
is a W*-Gray-category, we have that
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}({\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}})
forms a W*-Gray-category of normal
{\dagger}
-3-functors from
{\mathcal{A}}
to
{\mathcal{B}}
.

Theorem 3.24
.

The Yoneda embedding for a cubical C*-3-category
{\mathcal{B}}
, given on objects by

B\mapsto{\mathcal{B}}(-\to B),

can be equipped with the structure of monic
{\dagger}
-3-functor

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}\colon{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}({\mathcal{B}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\operatorname{\cccirc\!p}}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\Strict}),

which is locally a 2-equivalence. When
{\mathcal{B}}
is a W*-3-category, we obtain a monic normal
{\dagger}
-3-functor

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}\colon{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}({\mathcal{B}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\operatorname{\cccirc\!p}}}\to{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\Strict}).

Theorem 3.25

(Coherence for operator 3-categories)
.

Every C*-3-category is 3-equivalent to a C*-Gray-category, and every W*-3-category is 3-equivalent to a W*-Gray-category.

Proof.

By Theorem
3.21
and Theorem
3.24

{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathcal{A}}^{\cubical}\xhookrightarrow{\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}}{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}\big(({\mathcal{A}}^{\cubical})^{\scriptscriptstyle{\operatorname{\cccirc\!p}}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\Strict}\big)

where the latter is a C*-Gray-category by Proposition
3.23
.
∎

Theorem 3.26

(Gelfand-Naimark for operator 3-categories)
.

Every small C*-3-category is 3-equivalent to a sub-C*-Gray-category of
\mathsf{Hilb^{W^{*}2}}
.

Proof.

Notice there is a monic
{\dagger}
-3-functor
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}
given on objects by

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}(B)=\coprod_{A\in{\mathcal{A}}}{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B).

By extending the GNS construction for C*-2-categories into a monic
{\dagger}
-3-functor
\GNS\colon{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}\to\mathsf{Hilb^{W^{*}2}}
, the image of the composition

{\mathcal{A}}\xhookrightarrow{\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}}{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}\xhookrightarrow{\GNS}\mathsf{Hilb^{W^{*}2}}

is equivalent to
{\mathcal{A}}
.
∎

Appendix A
Background on operator categories

In this section, we provide the requisite background for operator 1-categories in the style of Section §
2
.

A.1
Operator categories

Definition A.1

(Operator category)
.

A C*-category is a
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear category
{\mathcal{A}}
equipped with:

(
{\dagger}
)

a conjugate linear contravariant involution
{\dagger}
fixing objects, and

(C*)

a sub-multiplicative norm
\|\cdot\|
satisfying the C*-axiom.

As a technical condition, we require algebraically positive morphisms to be spectrally positive, i.e.

(
\geq 0
)

for
f\in{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
, there exists
g\in\End(A)
such that
f^{\dagger}\circ f=g^{\dagger}\circ g
.

The previous condition holds automatically when
{\mathcal{A}}
admits direct sums. We further say that a C*-category
{\mathcal{A}}
is W* if:

(W*)

every hom-space
{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
admits a predual
{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)_{*}
, i.e. a Banach space with

{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)_{*}^{*}\cong{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B).

It automatically follows that
\circ
is separately weak*-continuous.

Example A.2

(Concrete operator 1-categories)
.

The prototypical example of an operator category is
\mathsf{Hilb}
, the W*-category of Hilbert spaces and bounded linear transformations. Here, the norm of a morphism is given by the operator norm and the dagger of a morphism is given by its adjoint. The predual

\mathsf{Hilb}(A\to B)_{*}

of the space of bounded linear transformations between the Hilbert spaces
A
and
B
is given by the following Banach space of trace-class operators

{\mathcal{L}}_{1}(B\to A)\coloneqq\{x\colon B\to A\mid\Tr(|x|)\leq\infty\}

after quotienting out by the negligibles of
\|\cdot\|\coloneqq\Tr(|\cdot|)
.
4
4

4

Here
|x|
denotes the square root
(x^{\dagger}x)^{1/2}
of the element
x^{\dagger}\circ x
in the C*-algebra
\End(A)
.

Furthermore, every norm-closed linear subcategory of
\mathsf{Hilb}
forms a C*-category, while every WOT-closed subcategory of
\mathsf{Hilb}
forms a W*-category. We call such operator 1-categories
concrete
.

The previous example motivates the following notion of an isomorphism compatible with an involution.

Definition A.3

(Unitaries)
.

We say that a morphism
x
in an operator category is unitary when
x^{\dagger}=x^{-1}
.

Definition A.4

((Normal)
{\dagger}
-functor)
.

A
{\dagger}
-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
between C*-categories is a linear functor which is
{\dagger}
-preserving, i.e.

F(x)^{\dagger}=F(x^{\dagger})\qquad\text{for }x\in{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B).

Furthermore, we say that a
{\dagger}
-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
between W*-categories is
normal
when it is weak*-continuous on each hom-space.

Definition A.5

(Uniformly bounded natural transformations)
.

We say that a natural transformation
\alpha\colon F\Rightarrow G
between
{\dagger}
-functors
F,G\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
is
uniformly bounded
when

\|\alpha\|\coloneqq\sup_{A\in{\mathcal{A}}}\|\alpha_{A}\|<\infty.

Operator 1-categories
are
1-categories of operators on Hilbert spaces. This is made precise by the Gelfand-Naimark theorem
[
GN43
]
and, in particular, the Gelfand-Naimark-Segal (GNS) construction
[
Seg47
]
. The following categorification of the usual concreteness result for algebras was shown by
[
GLR85
]
. We also note that the GNS construction involves the Yoneda embedding for an operator category.

Theorem A.6

(GNS)
.

Every small C*-category
{\mathcal{A}}
admits an embedding
5
5

5

By embedding, we mean injective on objects and faithful

{\dagger}
-functor

\Upsilon\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathsf{Hilb}.

This construction satisfies the following property:

•

For objects
A,B\in{\mathcal{A}}
and a functional
\varphi\in{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)^{*}
, there exist
\xi\in\Upsilon(A)
and
\eta\in\Upsilon(B)

such that

\varphi(x)=\langle\Upsilon(x)\xi,\eta\rangle,\quad\text{ for all }x\in{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B).

In this case we will write
\varphi=\langle\Upsilon\cdot\xi,\eta\rangle
.

Moreover, when
{\mathcal{A}}
is a small W*-category, there exists a normal embedding
{\dagger}
-functor
\Upsilon^{{\mathrm{W}}^{*}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathsf{Hilb}
.

Definition A.7
.

We define the C*-category
\GNS({\mathcal{A}})
to be the image of
\Upsilon
in
\mathsf{Hilb}
, for which
\Upsilon\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to\GNS({\mathcal{A}})
is an isomorphism of C*-categories. Moreover, we call operator subcategories of
\mathsf{Hilb}
concrete.

Definition A.8

(Bicommutant)
.

Given any C*-subcategory
{\mathcal{A}}
of
\mathsf{Hilb}
, we may take its WOT-closure in
\mathsf{Hilb}
, a W*-category which we will call the bicommutant
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime\prime}
of
{\mathcal{A}}
.

Theorem A.9

(Kaplansky Density Theorem)
.

For a subset
S\subset\mathsf{Hilb}(A\to B)
, if
x\colon A\to B
is in the SOT-closure of
A
, then there exist
(x_{\lambda})\subset S
with
\|x_{\lambda}\|\leq\|x\|
such that
x_{\lambda}\to x
SOT.

A.2
Tensor products of C*-categories

Definition A.10
.

For operator 1-categories
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, the algebraic tensor product
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
is a linear category equipped with a dagger
{\dagger}
consisting of:

(0)

objects
(A_{1},A_{2})
where
A_{i}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
, and

(1)

morphisms are given by
({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})((A_{1},A_{2})\to(B_{1},B_{2}))\coloneqq{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(A_{1}\to B_{1})\otimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(A_{2}\to B_{2})
.

Composition and
{\dagger}
are determined on each tensorand.

Definition A.11
.

For small C*-categories, the
minimal tensor product

{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\mintimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
is the completion of
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
on each hom-space with respect to the spatial
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}
-norm

\|t\|_{\sigma}\coloneqq\|(\Upsilon_{1}\otimes\Upsilon_{2})(t)\|

where
\Upsilon_{i}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{i}\to\mathsf{Hilb}
is the universal embedding of
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
as seen in Theorem
A.6
.

Definition A.12
.

The
maximal tensor product

{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
is the completion of
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
on each hom-space with respect to the maximal C*-norm

\|t\|_{\mu}\coloneqq\sup\left\{\|Ft\|\,\middle|\,F\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to\mathsf{Hilb}\text{ is a }{\dagger}\text{-functor}\right\}.

Universal Property A.13
.

For C*-categories
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, if
H\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
is a
*
-bilinear functor
6
6

6

By
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
we mean the cartesian product of the underlying categories, and by
{\dagger}
-bilinear functor we mean a functor which is linear and
{\dagger}
-preserving in each component.
into a C*-category
{\mathcal{B}}
, then there exists a unique
*
-functor
H\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
such that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}

Theorem A.14

(Hom-Tensor Adjunction)
.

For C*-categories
{\mathcal{A}}
,
{\mathcal{B}}
, and
{\mathcal{C}}
, we have that the following C*-categories are unitarily naturally equivalent:

{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}({\mathcal{A}}\maxtimes{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{C}})\cong{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}({\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}({\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{C}})).

Remark A.15
.

As was shown for C*-algebras by Takesaki and
Guichardet, there are many possible norms one can equip the algebraic tensor product
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
of small C*-categories such that completion yields a C*-category. It turns out that for any such norm
\|\cdot\|
, we have

\|\cdot\|_{\sigma}\leq\|\cdot\|\leq\|\cdot\|_{\mu}.

We refer the reader to Appendix T of
[
WO04
]
for an exposition on this topic.

A.3
W*-completion of a C*-category

The following section is a categorification of the results in
[
Are51b
]
and
[
Are51a
]
for C*-algebras. We refer the reader to
[
Pal74
]
for an exposition on the topic. As we will build on these facts to construct the W*-completion of a C*-2-category in Section
2.5
, we will provide sketches for some of our proofs concerning these results.

For a C*-category
{\mathcal{A}}
, we wish to construct the enveloping W*-category
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}({\mathcal{A}})
together with an embedding
{\dagger}
-functor
{\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{W}}^{*}({\mathcal{A}})
.
Intuitively, we wish to find the “smallest” C*-category containing
{\mathcal{A}}
whose hom-spaces admit preduals.
Recall that, for a C*-algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
, there exists an organic inclusion
\ev\colon{\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
given by

\ev_{x}(\varphi)\coloneqq\varphi(x)\quad\text{for }x\in{\mathcal{A}}\text{ and }\varphi\in{\mathcal{A}}^{*}.

Clearly
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
has a predual, namely
{\mathcal{A}}^{*}
, and the Goldstine theorem tells us that
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
is “small” in the following sense:

Theorem A.16

(Goldstine)
.

For a C*-algebra
7
7

7

This theorem holds more generally for any Banach space.

{\mathcal{A}}
,
\ev({\mathcal{A}})
is weak*-dense in
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
.

This leads us to the following construction.

Definition A.17

(Double dual of a C*-category)
.

When
{\mathcal{A}}
is a C*-category, we construct
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
as follows:

(0)

objects are the same as in
{\mathcal{A}}
,

(1)

morphisms are given by
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(A\to B)\coloneqq{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)^{**}
;

(
\circ
)

we define two so-called Arens compositions on
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
, which equip
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
with the structure of a (linear) category.

Remark A.18
.

Notice each
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(A\to B)
admits a vector space structure and a norm, namely,

\|\Phi\|\coloneqq\sup_{\varphi\in{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)^{*}}\frac{|\Phi(\varphi)|}{\|\varphi\|},\qquad\text{for }\Phi\in{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(A\to B).

Definition A.19

(Arens compositions)
.

For
\Phi\in{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(A\to B)
and
\Psi\in{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(B\to C)
, we define the left and right Arens compositions
\circ_{\ell}
and
\circ_{r}
as follows:

(
\ell
)

For
\varphi\in{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(A\to C)
, we set
(\Psi\circ\Phi)(\varphi)\coloneqq\Psi(\Phi\triangleright\varphi)
where
\Phi\triangleright\varphi\in{\mathcal{A}}(B\to C)^{*}
is given by:

(
\triangleright
)

For
b\in{\mathcal{A}}(B\to C)
, we set
(\Phi\triangleright\varphi)(b)\coloneqq\Phi(\varphi\triangleleft b)
where
\varphi\triangleleft b\in{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)^{*}
is given by:

(
\triangleleft
)

For
a\in{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
, we set
(\varphi\triangleleft b)(a)\coloneqq\varphi(b\circ a)
.

(
r
)

For
\varphi\in{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(A\to C)
, we set
(\Psi\circ_{r}\Phi)(\varphi)\coloneqq\Phi(\varphi\triangleleft\Psi)
where
\varphi\triangleleft\Psi\in{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)^{*}
is given by:

(
\triangleleft
)

For
a\in{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
, we set
(\varphi\triangleleft\Psi)(a)\coloneqq\Psi(a\triangleright\varphi)
where
a\triangleright\varphi\in{\mathcal{A}}(B\to C)^{*}
is given by:

(
\triangleright
)

For
b\in{\mathcal{A}}(B\to C)
, we set
(a\triangleright\varphi)(b)\coloneqq\varphi(b\circ a)
.

Definition A.20
.

We define
\ev\colon{\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
as follows:

(0)

For an object
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
,

\ev(A)\coloneqq A,

(1)

For
x\in{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
, we define
\ev(x)=\ev_{x}\in{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(A\to B)
by

\ev_{x}(\varphi)\coloneqq\varphi(x)\qquad\text{ for }\varphi\in{\mathcal{A}}(A\to{\mathcal{B}}).

Lemma A.21
.

\ev\colon{\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
is
{\dagger}
-preserving when we equip
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
with either Arens composition.

Definition A.22

(
{\dagger}
)
.

We define a conjugate-linear contravariant map
{\dagger}\colon{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(A\to B)\to{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(B\to A)
as follows:

(
{\dagger}
)

For
\Phi\in{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(A\to B)
, we define
\Phi^{\dagger}\in{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(B\to A)
by

\Phi^{\dagger}(\varphi)\coloneqq\overline{\Phi(\varphi^{\dagger})}\quad\text{for }\varphi\in{\mathcal{A}}(B\to A)^{*},

where
\varphi^{\dagger}\in{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)^{*}
is given by
\varphi^{\dagger}(a)\coloneqq\overline{\varphi(a^{\dagger})}
for
a\in{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
.

We now relate the Arens compositions via the following identity, which follows from a straightforward computation.

Lemma A.23
.

For
\Phi\in{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(A\to B)
and
\Psi\in{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(B\to C)
,

(\Psi\circ_{\ell}\Phi)^{\dagger}=\Phi^{\dagger}\circ_{r}\Psi^{\dagger}.

From our previous result, we see that if the Arens compositions on
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
agree then:

•

(\Psi\circ\Phi)^{\dagger}=\Phi^{\dagger}\circ\Psi^{\dagger}
where
\circ=\circ_{\ell}=\circ_{r}
and

•

\ev\colon{\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
is a
{\dagger}
-functor by
A.21
.

It turns out this is always the case for C*-categories, after which one proves that
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
satisfies the universal property required of the W*-completion of
{\mathcal{A}}
.

Theorem A.24
.

For a C*-category
{\mathcal{A}}
, the left and right Arens compositions on
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
coincide.
Furthermore, these serve to equip
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
with the structure of a W*-category.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, one assumes
{\mathcal{A}}
is small since compositions coincide if and only if they agree on each small subcategory. We first extend the universal representation
\Upsilon\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathsf{Hilb}
along
\ev

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Hilb}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}^{**}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Upsilon}}

by declaring that for each
\Phi\in{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(A\to B)
, we have

\langle\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Phi)\xi,\eta\rangle=\Phi(\langle\Upsilon\cdot\xi,\eta\rangle)\quad\text{for all }\xi\in\Upsilon(A)\text{ and }\eta\in\Upsilon(B).

In particular, this determines a bounded operator
\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Phi)\colon\Upsilon(A)\to\Upsilon(B)
with
\|\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Phi)\|\leq\|\Phi\|
.
Since every functional
\varphi\in{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)^{*}
is of the form
\varphi=\langle\Upsilon\,\cdot\,\xi,\eta\rangle
, it follows that
\Phi=0
if and only if
\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Phi)=0
.
For
\Phi\in{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(A\to B)
and
\Psi\in{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(B\to C)
, one computes

\langle\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Psi\circ_{\ell}\Phi)\xi,\eta\rangle=\langle\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Psi)\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Phi)\xi,\eta\rangle=\langle\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Psi\circ_{r}\Phi)\xi,\eta\rangle

which implies
\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Psi\circ_{\ell}\Phi)=\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Psi)\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Phi)=\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Psi\circ_{r}\Phi),
and hence
\circ_{\ell}=\circ_{r}
.

By Lemma
A.23
we obtain that
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
is a
{\dagger}
-category. Since
\mathsf{Hilb}
is an operator category, to show
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
is a C*-category it suffices to show that
\widetilde{\Upsilon}
is an isometric
{\dagger}
-functor. We then compute

\langle\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Phi^{\dagger})\xi,\eta\rangle=\langle\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Phi)^{\dagger}\xi,\eta\rangle,

from which we see that
\widetilde{\Upsilon}
is
{\dagger}
-preserving.

We now verify that
\|\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Phi)\|=\|\Phi\|
for
\Phi\in{\mathcal{A}}^{**}(A\to B)
.
By construction, it is clear that
\widetilde{\Upsilon}\colon{\mathcal{A}}^{**}\to\mathsf{Hilb}
is weak*-WOT continuous.
Let
\varepsilon>0
.
As a property of the
GNS construction
for
{\mathcal{A}}
, we know there exist
\xi\in\Upsilon(A),\eta\in\Upsilon(B)
with
\|\langle\Upsilon\cdot\xi,\eta\rangle\|=1
such that

|\langle\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Phi)\xi,\eta\rangle|=|\Phi(\langle\Upsilon\cdot\xi,\eta\rangle)|\geq\|\Phi\|-\varepsilon.

Since
\widetilde{\Upsilon}
is weak*-WOT continuous,
\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Phi)\in\overline{\operatorname{Im}\Upsilon}^{\mathrm{WOT}}=\overline{\operatorname{Im}\Upsilon}^{\mathrm{SOT}}\subseteq\mathsf{Hilb}(\Upsilon A\to\Upsilon B)
. By the
Kaplansky density theorem
, there exist
(x_{\lambda})\subset{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
with
\|x_{\lambda}\|=\|\Upsilon(x_{\lambda})\|\leq\|\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Phi)\|
such that
\Upsilon(x_{\lambda})\to\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Phi)
SOT. Observe

\|\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Phi)\|\geq\|x_{\lambda}\|\geq|\langle\Upsilon(x_{\lambda})\xi,\eta\rangle|\to|\langle\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Phi)\xi,\eta\rangle|\geq\|\Phi\|-\varepsilon.

Since
\varepsilon\geq 0
was arbitrary,
\|\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\Phi)\|\geq\|\Phi\|
. We conclude that
\widetilde{\Upsilon}
is isometry, and hence
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
is a W*-category.
∎

Universal Property A.25
.

For every
{\dagger}
-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
into a W*-category
{\mathcal{B}}
, there exists a unique normal extension
\widetilde{F}\colon{\mathcal{A}}^{**}\to{\mathcal{B}}
making the following diagram commute.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}^{**}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!\widetilde{F}}

Definition A.26
.

For every
{\dagger}
-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
between C*-categories
{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}
, there exists a unique normal extension
F^{**}\colon{\mathcal{A}}^{**}\to{\mathcal{B}}^{**}
afforded by the universal property of
{\mathcal{A}}^{**}
, which makes the following diagram commute.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}^{**}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{B}}^{**}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!F^{**}}

Using the facts shown in the proof of Theorem
A.24
, one proves the following result.

Corollary A.27

(Sherman-​Takeda for C*-categories)
.

For a small C*-category
{\mathcal{A}}
, the
{\dagger}
-functor

\widetilde{\Upsilon}\colon{\mathcal{A}}^{**}\to\GNS({\mathcal{A}})^{\prime\prime}

constructed in Theorem
A.24
is an equivalence of W*-categories extending
\Upsilon\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to\GNS(A)
as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\GNS({\mathcal{A}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}^{**}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\GNS({\mathcal{A}})^{\prime\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Upsilon}}

A.4
W*-tensor product of W*-categories

The following result is a categorification of the results in
[
Dau72
]
for W*-algebras. As we will adapt all of these results for W*-2-categories in Section
2.6
, we will omit the details in this work. We however present the main result here for completeness.

Universal Property A.28
.

For W*-categories
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, there is a W*-category
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wmaxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
equipped with separately normal
*
-bilinear functor
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wmaxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
satisfying the following universal property:

•

For every separately normal
*
-bilinear functor
H\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
into a W*-category
{\mathcal{B}}
, there exists a unique normal
*
-functor
\overline{H}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wmaxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}}
such that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wmaxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{H}}

Remark A.29
.

More specifically, we construct
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wmaxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
as the quotient of
({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})^{**}
by the polar of so-called separately normal functionals on
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxtimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
.

Appendix B
Background on operator 2-categories

B.1
Coherence and concreteness for operator algebraic bicategories

Definition B.1
.

For a C*-2-category
{\mathcal{A}}
, the Yoneda embedding

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}:{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\operatorname{\ccirc\!p}}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}({\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat})

is the
{\dagger}
-2-functor given by:

(0)

For an object
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
, we define the
{\dagger}
-2-functor
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A}:{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}
by:

•

For an object
B\in{\mathcal{A}}
, we set the C*-category
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A}(B)\coloneqq{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
,

•

For a morphism
{}_{B}X_{C}
in
{\mathcal{A}},

we set the
{\dagger}
-functor

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A}(_{B}X_{C})\coloneqq-\ccirc_{B}X_{C}
,

•

For a 2-morphism
x\colon\phantom{}_{B}X_{C}\to\phantom{}_{B}X^{\prime}_{C}
,
we set the uniformly bounded natural transformation
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A}(x)\coloneqq-\ccirc_{B}x
. Notice
\|\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A}(x)\|=\|x\|
.

•

We define the tensorator component
(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A})^{2}_{X,X^{\prime}}:\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A}(X)\ccirc_{\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A}(C)}\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A}(X^{\prime})\Rightarrow\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A}(X\ccirc_{C}X^{\prime})
for two composable morphisms
{}_{B}X_{C}
and
{}_{C}X^{\prime}_{D}
in
{\mathcal{A}}
to be the natural unitary
(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A})^{2}_{X,X^{\prime}}\coloneqq\alpha_{-,X,X^{\prime}}^{{\dagger}}
.

•

We define the unitor component
(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A})^{1}:\mathrm{id}_{\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A}(B)}\Rightarrow\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A}(\mathrm{id}_{B})
for an object
B\in{\mathcal{A}}
to be the natural unitary
(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A})^{1}_{B}\coloneqq\rho^{\dagger}
.

(1)

For a morphism
{}_{A^{\prime}}Y_{A}
in
{\mathcal{A}}
, we define the
{\dagger}
-2-natural transformation
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{Y}\colon\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A}\Rightarrow\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A^{\prime}}
by:

•

For an object
B\in{\mathcal{A}}
, we set the
{\dagger}
-functor
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{Y}_{B}\coloneqq\phantom{}_{A^{\prime}}Y\ccirc_{A}-
.

•

For a morphism
{}_{B}X_{C}
in
{\mathcal{A}}
, we set the uniformly bounded natural transformation
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{Y}_{X}\coloneqq\alpha_{Y,-,X}^{\dagger}
.

(2)

For a 2-morphism
y\colon\phantom{}_{A^{\prime}}Y_{A}\Rightarrow\phantom{}_{A^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}_{A}
, we define the uniformly bounded modification
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{y}\colon\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{Y}\ttto\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{Y^{\prime}}
by:

•

For an object
B\in{\mathcal{A}}
, we set the uniformly bounded natural transformation
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{y}_{B}\coloneqq y\ccirc_{A}-
.

(
\ccirc
)

We define the compositor component
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{2}_{Y^{\prime},Y}:\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{Y^{\prime}}\ccirc\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{Y}\Rightarrow\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{Y^{\prime}\ccirc Y}
for two composable morphisms
{}_{A^{\prime\prime}}Y^{\prime}_{A^{\prime}}
and
{}_{A^{\prime}}Y_{A}
in
{\mathcal{A}}
to be
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{2}_{Y^{\prime},Y}\coloneqq\alpha_{Y^{\prime},Y,-}
,

(I)

We define the unitor component
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{1}_{A}:\mathrm{id}_{\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{A}}\Rightarrow\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\mathrm{id}_{A}}
for an object
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
to be
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{1}_{A}\coloneqq\lambda^{\dagger}
.

Remark B.2
.

When
{\mathcal{A}}
is a strict C*-2-category, it is clear from construction that the Yoneda embedding for
{\mathcal{A}}
is a strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor which lands in
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\mathsf{st}}({\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat})
.

Theorem B.3
.

For a C*-2-category
{\mathcal{A}}
, the Yoneda embedding
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}:{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\operatorname{\ccirc\!p}}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}({\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat})
is fully faithful. Hence, every C*-2-category is equivalent to a strict one.

Proof.

This follows by the Yoneda embedding theorem for ordinary 2-categories.
∎

Theorem B.4
.

For a C*-2-category
{\mathcal{A}}
, the universal representation
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}\in{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}({\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat})
given by

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}:=\coprod_{A\in{\mathcal{A}}}\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{A}

is monic. Thus, every C*-2-category can be realized as a norm closed
{\dagger}
-2-subcategory of
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}
. Moreover

•

when
{\mathcal{A}}
is a strict,
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}\in{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}({\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat})
is a strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor; and

•

when
{\mathcal{A}}
is small, we have that
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}\in{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}({\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}_{\Small})
.

Proof.

To see that
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}
is injective, suppose
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}(A)=\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}(B)
for
A,B\in{\mathcal{A}}
. Then
\mathrm{id}_{A}\in\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}(B)
, which occurs only when
A=B
. To see
F
is injective on 1-morphisms, suppose
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}(_{A}X_{B})=\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}(_{A}X^{\prime}_{B})
and observe

{}_{A}X_{B}=\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}(_{A}X_{B})(\mathrm{id}_{A})=\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}(_{A}X^{\prime}_{B})(\mathrm{id}_{A})=\phantom{}_{A}Y_{B}.

An identical argument yields that
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}
is injective at level of 2-morphisms. Furthermore, when
{\mathcal{A}}
is strict,
is a strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor, hence
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}
is strict as well. Finally, when
{\mathcal{A}}
is small, the objects
B\in{\mathcal{A}}
form a set and each hom C*-category
{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)
is small, so

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}(B)=\coprod_{A\in{\mathcal{A}}}{\mathcal{A}}(A\to B)

is a small C*-category for every
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
.
∎

Definition B.5
.

Recall that, for a small C*-category
{\mathcal{B}}
, there exists a universal representation

{\mathcal{B}}\xrightarrow[\sim]{\Upsilon}\GNS({\mathcal{B}})\hookrightarrow\GNS({\mathcal{B}})^{\prime\prime}

where
\widetilde{\Upsilon}:{\mathcal{B}}^{**}\xrightarrow{\sim}\GNS({\mathcal{B}})^{\prime\prime}
is an isomorphism by Corollary
A.27
. We may upgrade this construction to a
{\dagger}
-2-functor

\GNS^{\prime\prime}\colon{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}_{\Small}\to\mathsf{Hilb^{W^{*}}}

as follows:

(0)

On a small C*-category
{\mathcal{B}}
,
\GNS^{\prime\prime}({\mathcal{B}})=\GNS({\mathcal{B}})^{\prime\prime}
.

(1)

For a
{\dagger}
-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{B}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{2}
between small C*-categories,
we define the normal
{\dagger}
-functor

\GNS^{\prime\prime}(F)\colon\GNS({\mathcal{B}}_{1})^{\prime\prime}\to\GNS({\mathcal{B}}_{2})^{\prime\prime}

as follows:

(F0)

On a Hilbert space
\Upsilon B\in\GNS({\mathcal{B}}_{1})^{\prime\prime}
where
B\in{\mathcal{B}}_{1}
, we set

\GNS^{\prime\prime}(F)(\Upsilon B)\coloneqq\Upsilon(FB)\in\GNS({\mathcal{B}}_{2})^{\prime\prime}.

(F1)

On an operator
S\in\GNS({\mathcal{B}}_{1})^{\prime\prime}(\Upsilon B\to\Upsilon B^{\prime})
, consider
\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{-1}S\in{\mathcal{B}}_{1}^{**}(B\to B^{\prime})
. As seen in Definition
A.26
, consider the morphism
F^{**}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{-1}S\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}^{**}(FB\to FB^{\prime})
. We then define the morphism
\GNS^{\prime\prime}(F)(S)\in\GNS({\mathcal{B}}_{2})^{\prime\prime}(\Upsilon FB\to\Upsilon FB^{\prime})
by

\GNS^{\prime\prime}(F)(S)\coloneqq\widetilde{\Upsilon}F^{**}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{-1}S.

Notice
\GNS^{\prime\prime}(F)
is a normal
{\dagger}
-functor since
\widetilde{\Upsilon}
and
F^{**}
are normal
{\dagger}
-functors. Moreover,
\GNS^{\prime\prime}
is strictly 1-composition-preserving since
(-)^{**}
is strict.

(2)

For a uniformly bounded natural transformation
\alpha\in{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}_{\Small}(\mbox{}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{1}}{F}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}}\tto\mbox{}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{1}}{G}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}})
, we define the uniformly bounded natural transformation

\GNS^{\prime\prime}(\alpha)\colon\GNS^{\prime\prime}(F)\Rightarrow\GNS^{\prime\prime}(G)

as follows:

(
\alpha 0
)

On a Hilbert space
\Upsilon B\in\GNS({\mathcal{B}}_{1})^{\prime\prime}
where
B\in{\mathcal{B}}_{1}
, we define the component

\GNS^{\prime\prime}(\alpha)_{\Upsilon B}\colon\Upsilon(FB)\to\Upsilon(GB)

in
\GNS({\mathcal{B}}_{2})^{\prime\prime}
by

\GNS^{\prime\prime}(\alpha)_{\Upsilon B}\coloneqq\Upsilon(\alpha_{B})=\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\ev_{\alpha_{B}}).

Lemma B.6
.

\GNS^{\prime\prime}\colon{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}_{\Small}\to\mathsf{Hilb^{W^{*}}}
is monic.

Proof.

Quite pedantically, it is clear that
\GNS^{\prime\prime}
is injective on objects. Now consider
{\dagger}
-functors
F,G\colon{\mathcal{B}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{2}
between small C*-categories such that
\GNS^{\prime\prime}(F)=\GNS^{\prime\prime}(G)
. Then, for every
B\in{\mathcal{B}}_{1}
, we have
\Upsilon(FB)=\Upsilon(GB)
, which implies
FB=GB
since
\Upsilon\colon{\mathcal{B}}_{2}\to\mathsf{Hilb}
is a monic
{\dagger}
-functor. For
b\in{\mathcal{B}}_{1}(B\to B^{\prime})
, consider
\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\ev_{b})\in\GNS({\mathcal{B}}_{1})^{\prime\prime}(\Upsilon B\to\Upsilon B^{\prime})
and observe

\widetilde{\Upsilon}F(b)=\widetilde{\Upsilon}F^{**}\ev_{b}=\GNS^{\prime\prime}(F)(\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\ev_{b}))=\GNS^{\prime\prime}(G)(\widetilde{\Upsilon}(\ev_{b}))=\widetilde{\Upsilon}G(b).

Thus
F=G
and we conclude
\GNS^{\prime\prime}
is injective on 1-morphisms. Finally, consider
\alpha\in{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}(F\Rightarrow G)
between arbitrary
{\dagger}
-functors
F,G
such that
\GNS^{\prime\prime}(\alpha)=0
. For
B\in{\mathcal{B}}_{1}
, observe

\Upsilon(\alpha_{B})=\GNS^{\prime\prime}(\alpha)_{\Upsilon B}=0,

hence
\alpha_{B}=0
since
\Upsilon:{\mathcal{B}}_{2}\to\mathsf{Hilb}
is a monic
{\dagger}
-functor. Therefore
\alpha=0
and we conclude that
\GNS^{\prime\prime}
is injective on 2-morphisms.
∎

Theorem B.7

(Gelfand-Naimark for C*-2-categories)
.

For a small C*-2-category
{\mathcal{A}}
, the universal representation

\Upsilon_{2}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathsf{Hilb^{W^{*}}}

given by
\Upsilon_{2}\coloneqq\GNS^{\prime\prime}\circ\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}
is monic. Thus, every C*-2-category
{\mathcal{A}}
can be realized as a norm-closed
{\dagger}
-2-category
\GNS_{2}({\mathcal{A}})\coloneqq\operatorname{Im}\Upsilon_{2}
of weakly closed
\dagger
-categories of Hilbert spaces and operators. Moreover, if
{\mathcal{A}}
is strict, then
\Upsilon_{2}
is a strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor.

Proof.

Both
{\dagger}
-functors
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}
and
\GNS^{\prime\prime}\colon{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}\to\mathsf{Hilb^{W^{*}}}
are monic, so
\Upsilon_{2}
is monic. Recall that
\GNS^{\prime\prime}
is always strict, whereas
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}
is strict whenever
{\mathcal{A}}
is. Therefore
\Upsilon_{2}
is a strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor if
{\mathcal{A}}
is a strict C*-2-category.
∎

B.2
Cofibrant replacement for operator 2-categories

Proposition B.8
.

For every C*-2-category
{\mathcal{A}}
, there exists a strict C*-2-category
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}
together with an epic 2-equivalence
\ev_{{\mathcal{A}}}\colon\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
. Moreover, when
{\mathcal{A}}
is a W*-2-category we have that
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}
is a W*-2-category and hence
\ev_{{\mathcal{A}}}
is automatically normal.

Proof.

We provide the construction in
[
Gur13
, §2.2.3]
, noting that all the relevant data is compatible with dagger structures.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be a C*-2-category.
We define the C*-2-category
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}
to have the same objects as
{\mathcal{A}}
, and free paths of 1-morphisms in
{\mathcal{A}}
as 1-morphisms.
More specifically,
X\in\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(A\to A^{\prime})
is some finite tuple

(A\xrightarrow{X_{0}}A_{1},\,A_{1}\xrightarrow{X_{1}}A_{2},\,\ldots,\,A_{n}\xrightarrow{X_{n}}A^{\prime})

of composable 1-morphisms in
{\mathcal{A}}
starting on
A
and ending on
A^{\prime}
.
We note that for every object
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
there is an empty string
\varnothing_{A}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(A\to A)
.
Composition
\widehat{\ccirc}
of 1-morphisms in
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}
is given by concatenation, which is strictly associative and unital with
\varnothing_{A}
acting as the identity on
{\mathcal{A}}
.

Before we define what 2-morphisms are in
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}
, we define
\ev_{\mathcal{A}}
on objects to act as the identity.
For
X\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(A\to A^{\prime})
we define

\ev_{\mathcal{A}}(X)=(\cdots((X_{0}\ccirc X_{1})\ccirc X_{2})\cdots)\ccirc X_{n},

that is,
\ev_{\mathcal{A}}
acts on a free path by evaluating its left-most parenthesization in
{\mathcal{A}}
.
In particular, when
X=\varnothing_{A}
, we have that
\ev_{\mathcal{A}}(\varnothing_{A})=\mathrm{id}_{A}
.
We may thus choose the unitor
\ev^{1}_{A}:\ev_{\mathcal{A}}(\varnothing_{A})\tto\mathrm{id}_{\ev_{\mathcal{A}}(A)}
to be
\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{A}}}
.
Note that
\ev_{\mathcal{A}}
is surjective on 1-morphisms since every 1-morphism
X_{0}\in{\mathcal{A}}(A\to A^{\prime})
forms a path
(X_{0})
of length 1.

For
X,Y\in\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(A\to A^{\prime})
, we now define the space of 2-morphisms from
X
to
Y
to be

\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(X\tto Y)\coloneqq{\mathcal{A}}(\ev_{\mathcal{A}}(X)\tto\ev_{\mathcal{A}}(Y)).

Notice that
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}
inherits compositions, linear and dagger structures, and norms from
{\mathcal{A}}
, equipping each
{\mathcal{A}}(A\to A^{\prime})
with the structure of a C*-category.
When
{\mathcal{A}}
is a W*-2-category, we further have that each
{\mathcal{A}}(A\to A^{\prime})
is a W*-category.

By setting
\ev_{\mathcal{A}}
to act as the identity on 2-morphisms, we immediately have that
\ev_{{\mathcal{A}}}
is locally a fully-faithful
\dagger
-functor.
For composable 1-morphisms
X=(X_{0},\ldots,X_{n})
and
Y=(Y_{0},\ldots,Y_{m})
in
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}
, we set the tensorator
\ev^{2}_{X,Y}:\ev_{\mathcal{A}}(X)\ccirc\ev_{\mathcal{A}}(Y)\tto\ev_{\mathcal{A}}(X\ccirc Y)
to be the unique coherence unitary

\big((\cdots(X_{0}\ccirc X_{1})\cdots)\ccirc X_{n}\big)\ccirc\big((\cdots(Y_{0}\ccirc Y_{1})\cdots)\ccirc Y_{m}\big)\;\tto\;(\cdots((((\cdots(X_{0}\ccirc X_{1})\cdots)\ccirc X_{n})\ccirc Y_{0})\ccirc Y_{1})\cdots)\ccirc Y_{m},

given by coherence for 2-categories.
The 1-composition
\widehat{\ccirc}
of composable 2-morphisms
a\in\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(X\tto Y)
and
a^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(X^{\prime}\tto Y^{\prime})
is given by the unique 2-morphisms in
a\,\widehat{\ccirc}\,a^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}((X\ccirc X^{\prime})\tto(Y\circ Y^{\prime}))
such that the following square commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\ev_{{\mathcal{A}}}(X)\ccirc\ev_{{\mathcal{A}}}(Y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\ev_{{\mathcal{A}}}(X\ccirc Y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\ev_{{\mathcal{A}}}(X^{\prime})\ccirc\ev_{{\mathcal{A}}}(Y^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\ev_{{\mathcal{A}}}(X^{\prime}\ccirc Y^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a\ccirc a^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev^{2}_{X,Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev^{2}_{X^{\prime},Y^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a\,\widehat{\ccirc}\,a^{\prime}}

This automatically implies that the tensorator
\ev^{2}_{X,Y}
is natural in
X
and
Y
.
By coherence for 2-categories it follows that
\ev_{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies all coherence axioms for
{\dagger}
-2-functors and
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}
satisfies all coherence axioms for C*-2-categories.
Finally, when
{\mathcal{A}}
is a W*-2-category, we see that
\widehat{\ccirc}
is separately normal since both
\circ
and
\ccirc
are separately normal in
{\mathcal{A}}
.
In this case we conclude that
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}
is also a W*-2-category and that
\ev_{\mathcal{A}}:\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
is automatically normal as an equivalence between W*-2-categories.
∎

Proposition B.9
.

For each
{\dagger}
-2-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
between C*-2-categories, there exists a strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor
\widehat{F}\colon\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{B}}}
and a unitary icon
u^{F}
as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{{\mathcal{B}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{{\mathcal{B}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{F}}

Moreover, when
F
is a normal
{\dagger}
-2-functor between W*-2-categories, then
\widehat{F}
is also normal.

Proof.

We provide the construction in
[
Gur13
, §2.3.3]
, noting that all the relevant data is compatible with dagger structures.
Let
F:{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
be a
{\dagger}
-2-functor between C*-2-categories.
We define
\widehat{F}
to act as
F
on objects, and on 1-morphisms by

\widehat{F}(A\xrightarrow{X_{0}}A_{1},\,A_{1}\xrightarrow{X_{1}}A_{2},\,\ldots,\,A_{n}\xrightarrow{X_{n}}A^{\prime})=(FA\xrightarrow{FX_{0}}FA_{1},\,FA_{1}\xrightarrow{FX_{1}}FA_{2},\,\ldots,\,FA_{n}\xrightarrow{FX_{n}}FA^{\prime}).

In particular
\widehat{F}(\varnothing_{A})=\varnothing_{FA}
, so
F
is strictly unital.
From construction it is clear that
\widehat{F}
is also strictly
\ccirc
-preserving.
For a 2-morphism
a\in\widehat{A}(X\tto Y)
we define
\widehat{F}(a)\in\widehat{{\mathcal{B}}}(\widehat{F}X\tto\widehat{F}Y)
to be the unique 2-morphism in
{\mathcal{A}}
such that the following diagram commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F((\cdots(X_{0}\ccirc X_{1})\cdots)\ccirc X_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\cdots(FX_{0}\ccirc FX_{1})\cdots)\ccirc FX_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F((\cdots(Y_{0}\ccirc Y_{1})\cdots)\ccirc Y_{m})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\cdots(FY_{0}\ccirc FY_{1})\cdots)\ccirc FY_{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\thicksim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\thicksim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{F}a}

Here the horizontal morphisms are the unique constraint unitaries provided by the coherence theorem for 2-functors
[
Gur13
, §2.3]
.
By this uniqueness it follows that
\widehat{F}
satisfies the coherence axioms for a strict 2-functor.
Moreover,
\widehat{F}
is
{\dagger}
-preserving since
F
is
{\dagger}
-preserving and the constraints for
F
are unitary.
From our definition of
\widehat{F}
on 2-morphisms, it is also clear that
\widehat{F}
is linear.
When
F
is a normal
{\dagger}
-2-functor between W*-2-categories, it follows that
\widehat{F}
is normal since
F
is and 2-composition
\circ
is separately normal.

Notice that
\ev_{\mathcal{B}}\circ\widehat{F}
and
\ev_{\mathcal{A}}\circ F
both act like
F
on objects, so they agree at the level of objects.
For
X=(X_{0},\ldots,X_{n})\in\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(A\to A^{\prime})
, we define the unitary

u^{F}_{X}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\big(\ev(FX_{0},\ldots,FX_{n}),F(\ev(X_{0},\ldots,X_{n}))\big)

to be the unique constraint unitary provided by the coherence theorem for 2-functors.
Note that
u^{F}_{X}
is natural in
X
since constraints are natural, and
u^{F}
satisfies the axioms of a unitary icon due to uniqueness of constraints.
∎

Proposition B.10
.

For C*-2-categories
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, there exists a cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor

C\colon\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}\maxblacktimes\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{2}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}

which is the identity on objects, and a unitary icon
u
as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}\maxblacktimes\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev\maxblacktimes\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\ev}}

Moreover, when
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
are W*-2-categories, we may upgrade
C
to a separately normal cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor

\overline{C}\colon\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}\Wblacktimes\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{2}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}

and
u
to a unitary icon
\overline{u}
as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}\Wblacktimes\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev\Wblacktimes\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{u}^{\ev}}

Proof.

We adapt
[
Gur13
, §8.1, Prop. 8.5]
.
To define
C
, we will provide the equivalent data seen in Proposition
2.14
.
For objects
A_{1}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
A_{2}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
we define

C_{A_{1}}(A_{2})=(A_{1},A_{2})=C_{A_{2}}(A_{1}).

Recall that we denote the identity of
A
in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
by
\mathrm{id}_{A}
, and its identity in
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}
by
\varnothing_{A_{1}}
.
For
X=(X_{0},\ldots,X_{n})\in\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{2}(A_{2}\to A_{2}^{\prime})
, we define

C_{A_{1}}(X)\coloneqq\big((\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}},X_{0}),\ldots,(\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}},X_{n})\big).

In particular
C_{A_{1}}(\varnothing_{A_{2}})=\varnothing_{(A_{1},A_{2})}
, so
C_{A_{1}}
is strictly unital.
It is clear from definition that
C_{A_{1}}
is also strictly
\ccirc
-preserving.
For
k\geq 0
, let
E^{k}_{1}
be the 1-morphism in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
given by

E^{k}_{1}\coloneqq\ev_{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}(\underbrace{\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}},\ldots,\mathrm{id}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}}_{k\text{ times}}).

For
a\in\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{2}(X\Rightarrow Y)
where
X=(X_{0},\ldots,X_{n})
and
Y=(Y_{0},\ldots,Y_{m})
, we define the 2-morphism

C_{A_{1}}(a)\in\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\big(C_{A_{1}}(X)\tto C_{A_{1}}(Y)\big)

as follows.
First note that
C_{A_{1}}(a)
must be a 2-morphism in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
with source
(E^{n+1}_{1},\ev_{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}(X))
and target
(E^{m+1}_{1},\ev_{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}(Y))
.
We may then define

C_{A_{1}}(a)\coloneqq\gamma_{n+1,m+1}\otimes a

where
\gamma_{n+1,m+1}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(E^{n+1}_{1}\tto E^{m+1}_{1})
is the unique constraint unitary given by coherence for 2-categories.
From this definition, it is immediate that
C_{A_{1}}
is linear.
Since
\upsilon_{n+1,m+1}^{\dagger}=\upsilon_{n+1,m+1}^{-1}=\upsilon_{m+1,n+1}
by uniqueness of constraints, we conclude that
C_{A_{1}}
is a strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor.
When
{\mathcal{A}}_{1},{\mathcal{A}}_{2},{\mathcal{B}}
are W*-2-categories and
C
is normal, we may identically define
\overline{C}_{A_{1}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}
, which is normal since
\otimes
is separately normal in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
.

One defines the (normal) strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor
F_{A_{2}}
in a similar fashion.
We now define the unitary
\Sigma_{X,Y}
for 1-morphisms
X=(X_{0},\ldots,X_{n})\in\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}(A_{1}\to A_{1}^{\prime})
and
Y=(Y_{0},\ldots,Y_{m})\in\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{2}(A_{2}\to A_{2}^{\prime})
as follows.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A_{1},A_{2})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A_{1},A_{2}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2}^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{A_{1}}(Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{A_{2}}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{A_{2}^{\prime}}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{A_{1}^{\prime}}(Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma_{X,Y}}

First note that
\Sigma_{X,Y}
must be a 2-morphism in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
with source

\ev_{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\big((\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}},Y_{1}),\ldots,(\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}},Y_{m}),(X_{1},\mathrm{id}_{A_{2}^{\prime}}),\ldots,(X_{n},\mathrm{id}_{A_{2}^{\prime}})\big)

and target

\ev_{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\big((X_{1},\mathrm{id}_{A_{2}}),\ldots,(X_{n},\mathrm{id}_{A_{2}}),(\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}^{\prime}},Y_{1}),\ldots,(\mathrm{id}_{A_{1}^{\prime}},Y_{m})\big).

We may then define
\Sigma_{X,Y}
to be the unique constraint unitary in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
given by coherence for 2-categories.
Note that
(\Sigma_{1})
follows by naturality of constraints, while
(\Sigma_{2})
and
(\Sigma_{3})
follows from their uniqueness. Hence, this data serves to determine a cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor

C\colon\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}\maxblacktimes\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{2}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}},

and in the W* case, a separately normal cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor

\overline{C}\colon\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}\Wblacktimes\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{2}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\Wblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}.

Note that
\ev\circ\,C
and
\ev\maxblacktimes\ev
act as the identity on objects, so they agree at the level of objects.
For a 1-morphism
(X,Y)
in
\widehat{A}_{1}\maxblacktimes\widehat{A}_{2}
where
X=(X_{0},\ldots,X_{n})
and
Y=(Y_{0},\ldots,Y_{m})
we define the unitary

u^{\ev}_{X,Y}\in({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\maxblacktimes{\mathcal{A}}_{2})\Big(\ev\big(F_{A_{1}}(Y)\ccirc F_{A_{2}}(X)\big)\tto\big(\ev(X),\ev(Y)\big)\Big)

as follows.
Notice

\ev\big(F_{A_{1}}(Y)\ccirc F_{A_{2}}(X)\big)=(E^{n+1}_{1}\ccirc\ev(X),\ev(Y)\ccirc E^{m+1}_{2})

We may then define
u^{\ev}_{X,Y}\coloneqq v_{1}\otimes v_{2}
where
v_{1}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(E^{n+1}_{1}\ccirc\ev(X)\tto\ev(X))
and
v_{2}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(\ev(Y)\ccirc E^{m+1}_{2}\tto\ev(X))
to be the unique constraint unitaries given by coherence for 2-categories.
We see that
u^{\ev}_{X,Y}
is natural in
X
and
Y
since constraints are natural, and
u
satisfies the axioms for unitary icons by uniqueness of constraints.
In the W* case, we define
\overline{u}^{\ev}
identically to
u^{\ev}
.
∎

Appendix C
Examples of operator algebraic tricategories:
\CHaus
and
\Meas

Example C.1
.

We define
\CHaus
to be the following C*-3-category:

1.

Objects are compact Hausdorff spaces;

2.

A morphism
A\colon X\to Y
is a C*-algebra equipped with
*
-homomorphisms
C(X)\to Z(A)
and
C(Y)\to Z(A)
;

3.

A 2-morphism
H\colon A\tto B
is an
A
-
B
C*-correspondence, i.e. a right B-Hilbert module
H
together with a left
A
-action
A\to{\mathcal{L}}_{B}(H)
, such that

ay\cdot h\cdot b=a\cdot h\cdot yb\quad\text{for }a\in A,y\in C(Y),h\in H,b\in B,

and a similar compatibility axiom for the
C(X)
-action.
Here
{\mathcal{L}}_{B}(H)
is the C*-algebra of adjointable
B
-intertwiner

4.

A 3-morphism
\varphi\colon H\to K
is an adjointable intertwiner, and define
\varphi^{\dagger}
to be its adjoint.

(
\circ
)

The composition
\circ
of 3-morphisms is given by composition of maps, which is clearly linear and
{\dagger}
-preserving.

(
\ccirc
)

The composition
\ccirc
of 2-morphisms
H\colon A\tto B
and
K\colon B\tto C
is given by the right C-Hilbert module

H\otimes_{B}K\coloneqq H\otimes K/\overline{\Span}(hb\otimes k-h\otimes bk:h\in H,b\in B,k\in K)

with
C
-valued inner product determined by

\langle h\otimes k,h^{\prime}\otimes k^{\prime}\rangle\coloneqq\langle k,\langle h,h^{\prime}\rangle\cdot k^{\prime}\rangle\quad\text{for }h,h^{\prime}\in H\text{ and }k,k^{\prime}\in K.

We determine the left action of
A
on
H\otimes_{B}K
by

a\cdot(h\otimes k)\coloneqq(a\cdot h)\otimes k,

which is well-defined since the action of
A
on
H
is given by right
B
-module homomorphisms. We define the right
C
-action on
H\otimes_{B}K
similarly. Furthermore, notice

\displaystyle ay\cdot(h\otimes k)\cdot c

\displaystyle=(ay\cdot h)\otimes(k\cdot c)

\displaystyle=(a\cdot h\cdot y)\otimes(k\cdot c)

\displaystyle=(a\cdot h)\otimes(y\cdot k\cdot c)

\displaystyle=(a\cdot h)\otimes(k\cdot yc)

\displaystyle=a\cdot(h\otimes k)\cdot yc,

and a similar argument shows the compatibility axiom for the
C(X)
-action.
Hence
H\ccirc_{B}K
satisfies the property required of 2-morphisms. The composition
\ccirc
of 3-morphisms
\varphi\colon\mbox{}_{A}{H}_{B}\ttto\mbox{}_{A}{H^{\prime}}_{B}
and
\psi\colon\mbox{}_{B}{K}_{C}\ttto\mbox{}_{B}{K^{\prime}}_{B}
is determined by

(\varphi\ccirc_{B}\psi)(h\otimes k)=\varphi(h)\otimes\psi(k)\quad\text{for }h\in H,k\in K.

This is well-defined on
H\otimes_{B}K
since

(\varphi\ccirc_{B}\psi)(hb\otimes k)=\varphi(h)b\otimes\psi(k)=\varphi(h)\otimes b\psi(k)=(\varphi\ccirc_{B}\psi)(h\otimes bk).

One similarly shows that
\varphi\ccirc_{B}\psi
is intertwiner. To see that it is also adjointable, observe

\displaystyle\langle(\varphi\ccirc_{B}\psi)(h\otimes k),h^{\prime}\otimes k^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\psi(k),\langle\varphi(h),h^{\prime}\rangle\cdot k^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle k,\langle h,\varphi^{\dagger}(h^{\prime})\rangle\cdot\psi^{\dagger}(k^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle h\otimes k,(\varphi^{\dagger}\otimes_{B}\psi^{\dagger})(h^{\prime}\otimes k^{\prime})\rangle

Hence
(\varphi\ccirc_{B}\psi)^{\dagger}=\varphi^{\dagger}\ccirc_{B}\psi^{\dagger}
. It is clear that
\ccirc
is bilinear from this definition.

(
\cccirc
)

We define the composition
\cccirc
of 1-morphisms
A\colon X\to Y
and
B\colon Y\to Z
to be

A\cccirc_{Y}B\coloneqq(A\maxtimes B)/\overline{\Span}(ay\otimes b-a\otimes yb:a\in A,y\in C(Y),b\in B),

where we note that
\overline{\Span}(ay\otimes b-a\otimes yb:a\in A,y\in C(Y),b\in B)
is a (norm-closed) two-sided ideal in
A\maxtimes B
since

\displaystyle(a^{\prime}\otimes b^{\prime})(ay\otimes b-a\otimes yb)

\displaystyle=a^{\prime}ay\otimes b^{\prime}b-a^{\prime}a\otimes b^{\prime}yb=a^{\prime}ay\otimes b^{\prime}b-a^{\prime}a\otimes yb^{\prime}b

\displaystyle(ay\otimes b-a\otimes yb)(a^{\prime}\otimes b^{\prime})

\displaystyle=aya^{\prime}\otimes bb^{\prime}-aa^{\prime}\otimes ybb^{\prime}=aa^{\prime}y\otimes bb^{\prime}-aa^{\prime}\otimes ybb^{\prime}.

In fact,
A\cccirc_{Y}B
is the coequalizer of
C(Y)\to A\to A\maxtimes B
and
C(Y)\to B\to A\maxtimes B
given by

A\cccirc_{Y}B=(A\maxtimes B)/\langle y\otimes 1_{B}-1_{A}\otimes y:y\in C(Y)\rangle.

We determine the maps
C(X)\to Z(A\ccirc_{Y}B)
and
C(Y)\to Z(A\ccirc_{Y}B)
by
x\mapsto x\otimes 1_{B}
and
y\mapsto 1_{A}\otimes y
respectively, which are clearly
*
-homomorphisms.

We define the composition
\cccirc
of 2-morphisms
H\colon\mbox{}_{X}{A}_{Y}\tto\mbox{}_{X}{A^{\prime}}_{Y}
and
K\colon\mbox{}_{Y}{B}_{Z}\tto\mbox{}_{Y}{B^{\prime}}_{Z}
to be the
(A\cccirc_{Y}B)
-
(A^{\prime}\cccirc_{Y}B^{\prime})
C*-correspondence with right
(A^{\prime}\cccirc_{Y}B^{\prime})
-Hilbert module

H\cccirc_{Y}K\coloneqq H\otimes K/\overline{\Span}(hy\otimes k-h\otimes yk:h\in H,y\in C(Y),k\in K).

Indeed, we equip
H\cccirc_{Y}K
with the
(A^{\prime}\cccirc_{Y}B^{\prime})
-valued inner product determined by

\langle h\otimes k,h^{\prime}\otimes k^{\prime}\rangle=\langle h,h^{\prime}\rangle\otimes\langle k,k^{\prime}\rangle.

Notice this is well-defined since

\displaystyle\langle hy\otimes k,h^{\prime}\otimes k^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle hy,h^{\prime}\rangle\otimes\langle k,k^{\prime}\rangle=\langle h,h^{\prime}\rangle y^{*}\otimes\langle k,k^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle h,h^{\prime}\rangle\otimes y^{*}\langle k,k^{\prime}\rangle=\langle h,h^{\prime}\rangle\otimes\langle k,k^{\prime}\rangle y^{*}

\displaystyle=\langle h,h^{\prime}\rangle\otimes\langle ky,k^{\prime}\rangle=\langle h,h^{\prime}\rangle\otimes\langle yk,k^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle h\otimes yk,h^{\prime}\otimes k^{\prime}\rangle.

We then determine the right
(A^{\prime}\cccirc_{Y}B^{\prime})
-action on
H\cccirc_{Y}K
by

(h\otimes k)\cdot(a^{\prime}\otimes b^{\prime})=ha^{\prime}\otimes kb^{\prime},

which is well-defined since

\displaystyle(h\otimes k)\cdot(a^{\prime}y\otimes b^{\prime})

\displaystyle=ha^{\prime}y\otimes kb^{\prime}=ha^{\prime}\otimes ykb^{\prime}=ha^{\prime}\otimes kyb^{\prime}=(h\otimes k)\cdot(a^{\prime}\otimes yb^{\prime})

\displaystyle(hy\otimes k)\cdot(a^{\prime}\otimes b^{\prime})

\displaystyle=hya^{\prime}\otimes kb^{\prime}=ha^{\prime}y\otimes kb^{\prime}=ha^{\prime}\otimes ykb^{\prime}=(h\otimes yk)\cdot(a^{\prime}\otimes b^{\prime}).

One determines the left
(A\cccirc_{Y}B)
-action similarly.

Finally, one determines the composition
\cccirc
of 3-morphisms akin to
\ccirc
and it is easy to show
\cccirc
is
{\dagger}
-preserving and bilinear.

We now define the tensorator (or interchanger) for
\cccirc
.
In particular, for
A,A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime}\colon X\to Y
,
B,B^{\prime},B^{\prime\prime}\colon Y\to Z
and
A\xrightarrow{H}A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{H^{\prime}}A^{\prime\prime}
,
B\xrightarrow{K}B^{\prime}\xrightarrow{K^{\prime}}B^{\prime\prime}
, we define a unitary

\Sigma\colon(H\cccirc_{Y}K)\ccirc_{A^{\prime}\cccirc_{Y}B^{\prime}}(H^{\prime}\cccirc_{Y}K^{\prime})\;\ttto\;(H\ccirc_{A^{\prime}}H^{\prime})\cccirc_{Y}(K\ccirc_{B^{\prime}}K^{\prime}),

determined by

(h\otimes k)\otimes(h^{\prime}\otimes k^{\prime})\mapsto(h\otimes h^{\prime})\otimes(k\otimes k^{\prime}).

A simple computation reveals that
\Sigma
is a well-defined intertwiner, clearly admitting an inverse intertwiner. Hence, to show
\Sigma
is unitary, it suffices to show it is isometric. Observe

\displaystyle\langle(h\otimes k)\otimes(h^{\prime}\otimes k^{\prime}),(\widetilde{h}\otimes\widetilde{k})\otimes(\widetilde{h}^{\prime}\otimes\widetilde{k}^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle h^{\prime}\otimes k^{\prime},\langle h\otimes k,\widetilde{h}\otimes\widetilde{k}\rangle\cdot\widetilde{h}^{\prime}\otimes\widetilde{k}^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle h^{\prime}\otimes k^{\prime},(\langle h,\widetilde{h}\rangle\otimes\langle k,\widetilde{k}\rangle)\cdot\widetilde{h}^{\prime}\otimes\widetilde{k}^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle h^{\prime}\otimes k^{\prime},(\langle h,\widetilde{h}\rangle\cdot\widetilde{h}^{\prime})\otimes(\langle k,\widetilde{k}\rangle\cdot\widetilde{k}^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle h^{\prime},\langle h,\widetilde{h}\rangle\cdot\widetilde{h}^{\prime}\rangle\otimes\langle k^{\prime},\langle k,\widetilde{k}\rangle\cdot\widetilde{k}^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle h\otimes h^{\prime},\widetilde{h}\otimes\widetilde{h}^{\prime}\rangle\otimes\langle k\otimes k^{\prime},\widetilde{k}\otimes\widetilde{k}^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle(h\otimes h^{\prime})\otimes(k\otimes k^{\prime}),(\widetilde{h}\otimes\widetilde{h}^{\prime})\otimes(\widetilde{k}\otimes\widetilde{k}^{\prime})\rangle.

On the other hand, the unitor for
\ccirc
is defined on components
\mathrm{id}_{A\cccirc_{Y}B}\tto\mathrm{id}_{A}\cccirc_{Y}\mathrm{id}_{B}
to be the identity unitary interchanger on
A\cccirc_{Y}B
viewed as a C*-correspondence over itself. Since the tensorator (interchanger) is morally a swap between the middle tensorands, it is quite easy to see that these satisfy the associativity and unitalily axioms for a
{\dagger}
-2-functor.

It is well-known that C*-algebras, C*-correspondences, and adjointable intertwiners form a C*-2-category, which we will denote by
\CstarAlg
.
[
CHPJP22
]

[
BLM04
]

[
Pas73
]

[
Rie74
]
One can slightly modify this argument to show that
\CHaus(X,Y)
is a C*-2-category for every
X,Y\in\CHaus
.

(I)

For an object
X\in\CHaus
, we set
I_{A}\coloneqq C(X)
where the maps
C(X)\to Z(C(X))
are given by the identity map on
C(X)
. We then extend
I_{A}\colon{\mathbb{C}}\to\CHaus(X\to X)
trivially into a
{\dagger}
-2-functor.

Recall that any (non-degenerate)
*
-homomorphism
\varphi\colon A\to B
between C*-algebras induces an
A-B
C*-correspondence
H_{\varphi}
given by:

•

The underlying vector space is
B
with
\langle b,b^{\prime}\rangle=b^{*}b^{\prime}
for
b,b^{\prime}\in B
.

•

The left
A
-action is given by

a\cdot b\coloneqq\varphi(a)b,

for
a\in A
and
b\in B
.

•

The right
B
-action is given by right multiplication in
B
.

When
\varphi
is unitary, there exists an induced unitary adjoint equivalence
(H_{\varphi},H^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}_{\varphi},\eta,\epsilon)
given by:

•

the
B
-
A
C*-correspondence
H^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}_{\varphi}\coloneqq H_{\varphi^{*}}
,

•

a unitary intertwiner
\eta\colon\mathrm{id}_{A}\to H_{\varphi}\ccirc_{B}H^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}_{\varphi}
defined as follows. Note that

b\otimes a=1\cdot\varphi(\varphi^{*}(b))\otimes a=1\otimes\varphi^{*}(b)\cdot a

in
H_{\varphi}\ccirc_{B}H^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}_{\varphi}
. Hence every element
\sum b_{i}\otimes a_{i}
in
H_{\varphi}\ccirc_{B}H^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}_{\varphi}
is of the form
1\otimes a
, and it is easy to show there is a unique such
a\in A
. We thus define
\eta
to be the bijection
a\mapsto 1\otimes a
. Notice
\eta
is an intertwiner

\displaystyle\eta(a)\cdot\widetilde{a}

\displaystyle=(1\otimes a)\cdot\widetilde{a}=1\otimes(a\widetilde{a})=\eta(a\widetilde{a})

\displaystyle\widetilde{a}\cdot\eta(a)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{a}(1\otimes a)=\varphi(\widetilde{a})\otimes a=1\otimes\varphi^{*}(\varphi(\widetilde{a}))a=1\otimes\widetilde{a}a=\eta(\widetilde{a}a),

and an isometry

\displaystyle\langle 1\otimes a,1\otimes\widetilde{a}\rangle=\langle a,\langle 1,1\rangle\cdot\widetilde{a}\rangle=\langle a,(1^{*}1)\cdot\widetilde{a}\rangle=\langle a,\widetilde{a}\rangle.

Therefore
\eta
is indeed a unitary intertwiner.

•

One defines the unitary intertwiner
\epsilon\colon H^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}_{\varphi}\ccirc_{B}H_{\varphi}\to\mathrm{id}_{B}
similarly by exchanging the roles of
A,B
and of
\varphi,\varphi^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}
.

A formal calculation reveals that
\epsilon
and
\eta
satisfies the zig-zag equations.

(a)

For composable 1-morphisms
\mbox{}_{X}{A}_{Y}
and
\mbox{}_{Y}{B}_{Z}
, The universal property of coequalizers yields an isomorphism
\varphi_{a}\colon A\cccirc_{Y}(B\cccirc_{Z}C)\to(A\cccirc_{Y}B)\cccirc_{Z}C
. This map is determined by
a\otimes(b\otimes c)\mapsto(a\otimes b)\otimes c
, and on easy verifies
\varphi_{a}
is unitary. We thus define the
A\cccirc(B\cccirc C)-(A\cccirc B)\cccirc C
-correspondence
a_{A,B,C}
to be
H_{\varphi_{a}}
and extend it to a unitary adjoint equivalence as mentioned previously.

We now define the naturality constraint for
a
. In particular, for
X\xrightarrow{A}Y\xrightarrow{B}Z\xrightarrow{C}W
,
X\xrightarrow{A^{\prime}}Y\xrightarrow{B^{\prime}}Z\xrightarrow{C^{\prime}}W
, and C*-correspondences
\mbox{}_{A}{H}_{A^{\prime}}
,
\mbox{}_{B}{K}_{B^{\prime}}
,
\mbox{}_{C}{L}_{C^{\prime}}
, we define a unitary

a_{HKL}\colon(H\cccirc(K\cccirc L))\ccirc a_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}C^{\prime}}\;\ttto\;a_{ABC}\ccirc((H\cccirc K)\cccirc L).

We note that that every element in
(H\cccirc(K\cccirc L))\ccirc a_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}C^{\prime}}
can be written as a sum of elements of the form

(h\otimes(k\otimes l))\otimes((1_{A^{\prime}}\otimes 1_{B^{\prime}})\otimes 1_{C^{\prime}}).

We then determine
a_{HKL}
to be the map

(h\otimes(k\otimes l))\otimes((1_{A^{\prime}}\otimes 1_{B^{\prime}})\otimes 1_{C^{\prime}})\mapsto((1_{A}\otimes 1_{B})\otimes 1_{C})\otimes((h\otimes k)\otimes l).

A simple computation reveals that
a_{HKL}
is a well-defined intertwiner, clearly admitting an inverse intertwiner.
To see that
a_{HKL}
is unitary, we will show it is isometric. Observe,

\displaystyle\langle(h\otimes(k\otimes l))\otimes((1\otimes 1)\otimes 1),(\widetilde{h}\otimes(\widetilde{k}\otimes\widetilde{l}))\otimes((1\otimes 1)\otimes 1)\rangle

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\langle(1\otimes 1)\otimes 1,\langle h\otimes(k\otimes l),\widetilde{h}\otimes(\widetilde{k}\otimes\widetilde{l})\rangle\cdot((1\otimes 1)\otimes 1)\rangle

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\langle(1\otimes 1)\otimes 1,(\langle h,\widetilde{h}\rangle\otimes\langle k,\widetilde{k}\rangle)\otimes\langle l,\widetilde{l}\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle(\langle h,\widetilde{h}\rangle\otimes\langle k,\widetilde{k}\rangle)\otimes\langle l,\widetilde{l}\rangle

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\langle(h\otimes k)\otimes l,\langle(1\otimes 1)\otimes 1,(1\otimes 1)\otimes 1\rangle((\widetilde{h}\otimes\widetilde{k})\otimes\widetilde{l})\rangle

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\langle((1\otimes 1)\otimes 1)\otimes((h\otimes k)\otimes l),((1\otimes 1)\otimes 1)\otimes((\widetilde{h}\otimes\widetilde{k})\otimes\widetilde{l})\rangle.

It is also clear from construction that
a_{HKL}
is natural in
H
,
K
, and
L
.

(u)

Notice
I_{X}\cccirc_{X}A=C(X)\cccirc_{X}A\cong A
for a 1-morphism
\mbox{}_{X}{A}_{Y}
.
Denoting this unitary by
\varphi_{\ell}
, we define the
(I_{X}\cccirc A)-A
C*-correspondence
\ell_{A}
to be
H_{\varphi_{\ell}}
, as extend it to a unitary adjoint equivalence as mentioned previously.
One defines the C*-correspondence
r_{A}=H_{\varphi_{r}}
similarly through a map
\varphi_{r}\colon A\cccirc_{Y}I_{Y}\to A
. For a C*-correspondence
\mbox{}_{A}{H}_{B}
between C*-algebras
A,B\colon X\to Y
, one determines the naturality constraint unitary interchangers

\displaystyle\ell_{X}\colon(I_{X}\cccirc H)\ccirc\ell_{B}\,

\displaystyle\ttto\,\ell_{A}\ccirc H

\displaystyle r_{X}\colon(H\cccirc I_{Y})\ccirc r_{B}\,

\displaystyle\ttto\,r_{A}\ccirc H

respectively by

\displaystyle(1_{C(X)}\otimes h)\otimes 1_{B}

\displaystyle\mapsto 1_{A}\otimes h,

\displaystyle(h\otimes 1_{C(Y)})\otimes 1_{B}

\displaystyle\mapsto 1_{A}\otimes h.

(
\pi
)

In what follows, we will supress the associators for
\ccirc
. For composable C*-algebras
A,B,C,D
, we determine the natural unitary interchanger

\pi_{ABCD}\colon(\mathrm{id}_{A}\cccirc a_{BCD})\ccirc a_{A,B\cccirc C,D}\ccirc(a_{ABC}\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{D})\;\ttto\;a_{A,B,C\cccirc D}\ccirc a_{A\cccirc B,C,D},

to be the map which sends

(1_{A}\otimes((1_{B}\otimes 1_{C})\otimes 1_{D}))\otimes((1_{A}\otimes(1_{B}\otimes 1_{C}))\otimes 1_{D})\otimes(((a\otimes b)\otimes c)\otimes d),

to

((1_{A}\otimes 1_{B})\otimes(1_{C}\otimes 1_{D}))\otimes(((a\otimes b)\otimes c)\otimes d).

(coh)

For composable C*-algebras
\mbox{}_{X}{A}_{Y}
and
\mbox{}_{Y}{B}_{Z}
, we define the middle, left, and right unity coheretor unitary intertwiners

\displaystyle\mu_{AB}
