\displaystyle\colon(\mathrm{id}_{a}\cccirc\ell^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}_{B})\ccirc a_{A,I_{Y},B}\ccirc(r_{A}\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{B})\;\ttto\;\mathrm{id}_{A\cccirc B}

\displaystyle\lambda_{AB}

\displaystyle\colon\ell^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}_{A}\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{B}\;\ttto\;\ell^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}_{A\cccirc B}\ccirc a_{I_{X},A,B}

\displaystyle\rho_{AB}

\displaystyle\colon\mathrm{id}_{A}\cccirc r_{B}\;\ttto a_{A,B,I_{Z}}\ccirc r_{A\cccirc B}

to be the maps determined by

\displaystyle(1_{A}\otimes(1_{C(Y)}\otimes 1_{B}))\otimes((1_{A}\otimes 1_{C(Y)})\otimes 1_{B})\otimes(a\otimes b)

\displaystyle\mapsto a\otimes b,

\displaystyle(1_{C(X)}\otimes a)\otimes b

\displaystyle\mapsto(1_{C(X)}\otimes(1_{A}\otimes 1_{B}))\otimes((1_{C(X)}\otimes a)\otimes b),

\displaystyle a\otimes b

\displaystyle\mapsto((1_{A}\otimes 1_{B})\otimes 1_{C(Z)})\otimes(a\otimes b).

The associativity condition for
\cccirc
compares two 3-morphisms with source

(\mathrm{id}_{A}\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{B}\cccirc a_{CDE})\ccirc(\mathrm{id}_{A}\cccirc a_{B,CD,E})\ccirc(\mathrm{id}_{A}\cccirc(a_{BCD}\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{E}))\ccirc a_{A,(BC)D,E}\ccirc(a_{A,BC,D}\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{E})\ccirc((a_{ABC}\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{D})\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{E}),

and target

a_{A,B,C(DE)}\ccirc a_{AB,C,DE}\ccirc a_{(AB)C,D,E}.

A formal calculation reveals that both 3-morphisms are determined by mapping

1_{A(B((CD)E))}\otimes 1_{A((B(CD))E)}\otimes 1_{A(((BC)D)E}\otimes 1_{(A((BC)D))E}\otimes 1_{(((AB)C)D)E}\otimes((((a\otimes b)\otimes c)\otimes d)\otimes e)

to

1_{(AB)(C(DE))}\otimes 1_{((AB)C)(DE)}\otimes((((a\otimes b)\otimes c)\otimes d)\otimes e)

where we denote
1_{A}\otimes(1_{B}\otimes((1_{C}\otimes 1_{D})\otimes 1_{E}))
by
1_{A(B((CD)E))}
and so on.

The two axioms relating the unity coheretors with the associators and pentagonator compare two 3-morphisms with sources

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id}_{A}\cccirc(\mathrm{id}_{B}\cccirc\ell^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}_{C}))\ccirc(\mathrm{id}_{A}\cccirc a_{B,I_{Z},C})\ccirc(\mathrm{id}_{A}\cccirc(r_{B}\cccirc B))\ccirc a_{ABC},

\displaystyle a_{ABC}\ccirc((\mathrm{id}_{A}\cccirc\ell^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}_{B})\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{C})\ccirc(a_{A,I_{Y},B}\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{C})\ccirc((r_{A}\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{B})\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{C}),

and targets

\displaystyle a_{ABC}\ccirc\mathrm{id}_{(AB)C},

\displaystyle\mathrm{id}_{A(BC)}\ccirc a_{ABC}

respectively. Another formal calculation reveals that both 3-morphisms are determined by mapping

\displaystyle 1_{A(B(I_{Z}C)}\otimes 1_{A((BI_{Z})C)}\otimes 1_{A(BC)}\otimes((a\otimes b)\otimes c),

\displaystyle 1_{(AB)C}\otimes 1_{(A(I_{Y}B))C}\otimes 1_{((AI_{Y})B)C}\otimes((a\otimes b)\otimes c),

into

\displaystyle 1_{(AB)C}\otimes((a\otimes b)\otimes c),

\displaystyle 1_{A(BC)}\otimes((a\otimes b)\otimes c),

respectively.

Example C.2
.

One defines the analogous W*-3-category
\Meas
whose:

•

objects are
\sigma
-finite measure spaces
(X,\mu
);

•

1-morphisms
A\colon(X,\mu)\to(Y,\nu)
are W*-algebras
A
equipped with normal
*
-homomorphisms
L^{\infty}(X,\mu)\to Z(A)
and
L^{\infty}(Y,\nu)\to Z(A)
;

•

2-morphisms
H\colon A\tto B
are
A
-
B
W*-correspondences compatible with the
L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
amd
L^{\infty}(Y,\nu)
actions,

•

3-morphisms are adjointable normal intertwiners.

Appendix D
Yoneda for operator algebraic tricategories

We will now construct the Yoneda embedding for a
cubical
C*-3-category
{\mathcal{B}}

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}\colon{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}({\mathcal{B}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\operatorname{\cccirc\!p}}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}),

and a corresponding normal version

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}\colon{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}({\mathcal{B}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\operatorname{\cccirc\!p}}}\to{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}),

when
{\mathcal{B}}
is a W*-3-category. We begin by constructing the targets of these
{\dagger}
-3-functors.

Lemma D.1
.

For C*-3-categories
{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}
, and
{\dagger}
-3-functors
F,G\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
, there exists an organic C*-2-category structure on
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}(\mbox{}_{{\mathcal{A}}}{F}_{{\mathcal{B}}}\tto\mbox{}_{{\mathcal{A}}}{G}_{{\mathcal{B}}})
, the 2-category of

(0)

{\dagger}
-3-natural transformations from
F
to
G
,

(1)

{\dagger}
-3-modifications, and

(2)

uniformly bounded perturbations.

which is strict whenever
{\mathcal{B}}
is locally strict.

Moreover, when
{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}
are W*-3-categories and
F,G
are normal
{\dagger}
-3-functors, we have that
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}(F\tto G)
forms a W*-3-category.

Proof.

By
[
Gur13
, §9.1, Theorem 9.1]
we know that all 3-natural transformations from
F
to
G
, together with all 3-modifications and perturbations between them form a 2-category
\mathsf{3Cat}(F\tto G)
where the components for the associators and unitors are given by the associators and unitors of the components in
{\mathcal{B}}
respectively.
First note that composites of unitary constraints for 3-natural transformations and 3-modifications are also unitary, so that
{\dagger}
-3-natural transformations,
{\dagger}
-3-modifications, and all perturbations form a 2-subcategory of
\mathsf{3Cat}(F\tto G)
.
Since linear combinations and compositions occur componentwise, it is clear that
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}(F\tto G)
(resp.
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}(F\tto G)
) forms a (linear) 2-subcategory of
\mathsf{3Cat}(F\tto G)
.
By defining
{\dagger}
to act componentwise on uniformly bounded perturbations, this yields a C*-2-category structure on
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}(F\tto G)
(resp.
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}(F\tto G)
).

In the W* case, one then uses the Kaplansky density theorem together with
[
CP22
, Lemma 2.13]
to show that
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}(F\tto G)
is locally a C*-category and that
\ccirc
is separately normal.
∎

Lemma D.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be a C*-3-category,
{\mathcal{B}}
a C*-Gray-category, and
F,G,H\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}

{\dagger}
-3-functors. Then 1-composition forms cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor

\cccirc\colon{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}(F\tto G)\maxblacktimes{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}(G\tto H)\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}(F\tto H).

Moreover, when
{\mathcal{A}}
is a W*-3-category,
{\mathcal{B}}
a W*-Gray-category, and
F,G,H
are normal
{\dagger}
-3-functors, we have that
\cccirc
is separately normal.

Proof.

One easily verifies that the underlying composite of
{\dagger}
-3-natural transformations,
{\dagger}
-3-modifications, and uniformly bounded perturbations is again of the respective type. By
[
Gur13
, Thm. 9.3]
, we then know that
\cccirc
assembles into a cubical 2-functor of the underlying 2-categories. It is also easy to see that
\cccirc
is
{\dagger}
-bilinear since linear combinations of perturbations are obtained componentwise, and that the interchanger
\Sigma
for
\cccirc
is unitary as it arises from the C*-Gray-category
{\mathcal{B}}
on each component of the relevant
{\dagger}
-3-modifications. Therefore
\cccirc
forms a cubical
{\dagger}
-2-functor.

In the W* case, since
\cccirc
is separately normal in
{\mathcal{B}}
, the Kaplansky density theorem together with
[
CP22
, Lemma 2.13]
yields that
\cccirc
is separately normal.
∎

Corollary D.3
.

For
{\mathcal{A}}
a C*-3-category and
{\mathcal{B}}
a C*-Gray-category,
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}({\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}})
forms a C*-Gray-category of

(0)

{\dagger}
-3-functors from
{\mathcal{A}}
to
{\mathcal{B}}
,

(hom)

Hom-C*-2-categories
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}(\mbox{}_{{\mathcal{A}}}{F}_{{\mathcal{B}}}\tto\mbox{}_{{\mathcal{A}}}{G}_{{\mathcal{B}}})
as in Lemma
D.1
,

(
\cccirc
)

Cubical 1-composition
\cccirc
as in Lemma
D.2

Moreover, when
{\mathcal{A}}
is a W*-3-category and
{\mathcal{B}}
is a W*-Gray-category, we have that
{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}({\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}})
forms a W*-Gray-category of normal
{\dagger}
-3-functors from
{\mathcal{A}}
to
{\mathcal{B}}
.

We now begin constructing the actual embedding.

Lemma D.4
.

For a cubical C*-3-category
{\mathcal{B}}
and an object
B\in{\mathcal{B}}
, there is an organic contravariant hom-
{\dagger}
-3-functor

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}\colon{\mathcal{B}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\operatorname{\cccirc\!p}}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\Strict}.

When
{\mathcal{B}}
is a cubical W*-3-category, this construction yields a normal
{\dagger}
-3-functor

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}\colon{\mathcal{B}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\operatorname{\cccirc\!p}}}\to{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\Strict}.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}
be a C*-3-category (resp. W*-3-category).
We will present the definition for the underlying Yoneda embedding for 3-categories seen in
[
Gur13
]
, noting that all the relevant data is compatible with dagger structures.

(0)

On an object
B^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}}
, we set

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(B^{\prime})\coloneqq{\mathcal{B}}(B^{\prime}\to B),

which is a strict C*-2-category (resp. W*-2-category) since
{\mathcal{B}}
is cubical.

(1)

On a 1-morphism
Y\in{\mathcal{B}}(B^{\prime\prime}\to B^{\prime})
, we define the strict (normal)
{\dagger}
-2-functor

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y)\colon\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(B^{\prime})\to\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(B^{\prime\prime})

as follows:

(1.0)

For an object
X\in\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(B^{\prime})={\mathcal{B}}(B^{\prime}\to B)
, which is a 1-morphism in
{\mathcal{B}}
, we set

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y)(X)\coloneqq\mbox{}_{B^{\prime\prime}}{Y}\cccirc_{B^{\prime}}\mbox{}{X}_{B}.

(1.1)

For a 1-morphism
b
in
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(B^{\prime})
, which is a 2-morphism in
{\mathcal{B}}
, we set

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y)(b)\coloneqq\mathrm{id}_{Y}\cccirc_{B^{\prime}}b.

(1.2)

Similarly, for a 2-morphism
\beta
in
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(B^{\prime})
, which is a 3-morphism in
{\mathcal{B}}
, we set

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y)(\beta)\coloneqq\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{Y}}\cccirc_{B^{\prime}}\beta.

Notice
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y)
is linear,
{\dagger}
-preserving, and preserves 3-composition
\circ
and identities at the level of 2-morphisms in
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(B^{\prime})
(which are 3-morphisms in
{\mathcal{B}}
). Moreover,
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y)
is strict since
\cccirc
is cubical.

In the W* case, since
\cccirc
is separately normal in
{\mathcal{B}}
, the Kaplansky density theorem together with
[
CP22
, Lemma 2.13]
yields that
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y)
is normal.

(2)

For a 2-morphism
b\in{\mathcal{B}}(\mbox{}_{B^{\prime\prime}}{Y}_{B^{\prime}}\tto\mbox{}_{B^{\prime\prime}}{Y^{\prime}\!}_{B^{\prime}})
, we define the
{\dagger}
-2-natural transformation

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(b)\colon\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y)\tto\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y^{\prime}).

as follows:

(2.0)

For an object
X\in\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(B^{\prime})
, we define the component

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(b)_{X}\colon\underbrace{\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y)(X)}_{Y\cccirc X}\to\underbrace{\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y^{\prime})(X)}_{Y^{\prime}\cccirc X}

to be
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(b)_{X}\coloneqq b\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{X}
.

(2.1)

For a 1-morphism
a
in
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(B^{\prime})
, we define the component unitary
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(b)_{a}
to be
\Sigma^{\dagger}_{a,b}
, which comes from the unitary interchanger for the cubical 1-composition
\cccirc
in
{\mathcal{B}}
.

(3)

On a 3-morphism
\beta\in{\mathcal{B}}(b\ttto b^{\prime})
, we define the uniformly bounded modification

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(\beta)\colon\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(b)\ttto\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(b^{\prime})

as follows:

(3.0)

For an object
X\in\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(B^{\prime})
, we define the component

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(\beta)_{X}\colon\underbrace{\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(b)_{X}}_{b\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{X}}\ttto\underbrace{\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(b^{\prime})_{X}}_{b^{\prime}\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{X}}

to be
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(\beta)_{X}\coloneqq\beta\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{X}}
.

Notice
\|\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(\beta)\|=\sup_{X}\|\beta\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{X}}\|\leq\|\beta\|
, so
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(\beta)
is indeed uniformly bounded.

From this definition it is clear that
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}
is linear,
{\dagger}
-preserving, and preserves 3-composition
\circ
and identities at the level of 3-morphisms.

In the W* case, since
\cccirc
is separately normal in
{\mathcal{B}}
, the Kaplansky density theorem together with
[
CP22
, Lemma 2.13]
yields that
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}
is normal.

Using cubicality, one can further check that
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}
is locally a strict (normal)
{\dagger}
-2-functor between strict C*-2-categories (resp.​ W*-2-categories). We now recall the construction for the constraint unitary adjoint equivalences
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{2}_{B}
and
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{0}_{B}
for the
{\dagger}
-3-functor
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}
:

(
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{2}
)

For composable 1-morphisms
\mbox{}_{B^{\prime\prime\prime}}{Y^{\prime}\!}_{B^{\prime\prime}},\mbox{}_{B^{\prime\prime}}{Y}_{B^{\prime}}
in
{\mathcal{B}}
, we provide a tensorator
{\dagger}
-2-natural transformation

(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{2})_{Y^{\prime},Y}\colon\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y^{\prime})\cccirc\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y)\tto\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y^{\prime}\cccirc_{B^{\prime\prime}}Y^{\prime}).

as follows:

(
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{2}.0
)

On an object
X\in\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(B^{\prime})
, we define the component

((\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{2})_{Y^{\prime},Y})_{X}\colon\underbrace{\big(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y^{\prime})\cccirc\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y)\big)(X)}_{Y^{\prime}\cccirc_{B^{\prime\prime}}(Y\cccirc_{B^{\prime}}X)}\tto\underbrace{\big(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y^{\prime}\cccirc_{B^{\prime\prime}}Y^{\prime})\big)(X)}_{(Y^{\prime}\cccirc_{B^{\prime\prime}}Y)\cccirc_{B^{\prime}}X},

to be
((\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{2})_{Y^{\prime}\!,Y})_{X}\coloneqq a_{Y^{\prime}\!,Y,X}
.

(
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{2}.1
)

For a 1-morphism
b
in
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(B^{\prime})
, we define the unitary

((\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{2})_{Y^{\prime}\!,Y})_{b}\coloneqq a_{\mathrm{id}_{Y^{\prime}},\mathrm{id}_{Y},b}.

One continues by defining the unitary adjoint equivalence for
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{2}
to be the unitary adjoint equivalence for
a
with the first two variables held constant.

(
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{0}
)

For an object
B^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}}
, we provide a unitor
{\dagger}
-2-natural transformation

(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{0}_{B})_{B^{\prime}}\colon\mathrm{id}_{\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(B^{\prime})}\tto\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(\mathrm{id}_{B^{\prime}}),

as follows:

(
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{0}.0
)

On an object
\mbox{}_{B^{\prime}}{X}_{B}\in\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(B^{\prime})
, we define the component

((\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{0})_{B^{\prime}})_{X}\colon X\tto\mathrm{id}_{B^{\prime}}\cccirc X,

to be
((\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{0})_{B^{\prime}})_{X}\coloneqq r^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}_{X}
. One continues defining the unitary adjoint equivalence for
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{0}_{B}
similarly to
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{2}_{B}
.

We now provide the associativity and unitality constraint unitaries
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{\alpha}
,
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\lambda}_{B}
, and
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\rho}_{B}
for the
{\dagger}
-3-functor
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}
:

(
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{\alpha}
)

We define the associator unitary
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{\alpha}

to be the following mate of
\pi
, the pentagonator for
{\mathcal{B}}
.

\pi
(1\cccirc a)
a^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}
a^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}
(a^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}\cccirc 1)
a^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}

Indeed,
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{\alpha}
is unitary as
\pi
is unitary, and the unit and counit for the unitary adjoint equivalence
\mathbf{\alpha}
are unitaries.

(
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{\lambda}
)

The left unitor unitary
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\lambda}_{B}
is given by a mate of
\rho
.

(
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}^{\rho}
)

The right unitor unitary
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\lambda}_{B}
is given by a mate of
\mu
.

We refer the interested reader to
[
Gur13
]
for the proof that
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}
satisfies both constraint axioms for a 3-functor.
∎

Lemma D.5
.

For a cubical C*-3-category
{\mathcal{B}}
and a 1-morphism
X\in{\mathcal{B}}(B\to B^{\prime})
, there is an organic
{\dagger}
-3-natural transformation

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X}\colon\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}\tto\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B^{\prime}}.

Proof.

We will present the definition found in
[
Gur13
, Lemma 9.8]
, noting that all the relevant data is compatible with dagger structures.

(0)

For an object
A\in{\mathcal{B}}
, we define the component

(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X})_{A}\colon\underbrace{\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(A)}_{{\mathcal{B}}(A\to B)}\to\underbrace{\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B^{\prime}}(A)}_{{\mathcal{B}}(A\to B^{\prime})},

to be the (normal) strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor given by:

(0.0)

For an object
\mbox{}_{A}{Y}_{B}\in\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(A)
,

(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X})_{A}(Y)\coloneqq\mbox{}_{A}{Y}\cccirc_{B}\mbox{}{X}_{B^{\prime}}.

(0.1)

For a 1-morphism
b
in
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(A)
,

(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X})_{A}(b)\coloneqq b\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{X}.

(0.2)

For a 2-morphism
\beta
in
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(A)
,

(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X})_{A}(b)\coloneqq\beta\cccirc\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{X}}.

(1)

For a 1-morphism
Y\in{\mathcal{B}}(A\to A^{\prime})
, we provide a unitary adjoint equivalence for
(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X})_{Y}
, where

(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X})_{Y}

is a
{\dagger}
-2-natural transformation given by:

(1.0)

On an object
\mbox{}_{A}{Z}_{B}\in\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(A)
, we define the component

(((\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X})_{Y})_{Z})\colon\underbrace{(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X})_{A^{\prime}}\cccirc\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(Y)(Z)}_{(\mbox{}_{A^{\prime}}{Y}\cccirc_{A}Z)\cccirc_{B}\mbox{}{X}_{B^{\prime}}}\tto\underbrace{\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B^{\prime}}(Y)\cccirc(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X})_{A}(Z)}_{\mbox{}_{A^{\prime}}{Y}\cccirc_{A}(Z\cccirc_{B}\mbox{}{X}_{B^{\prime}})}

to be
(((\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X})_{Y})_{Z})\coloneqq a_{YZX}^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}
.

(1.1)

On a 1-morphism
c
in
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(A)
, we set
(((\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X})_{Y})_{c}\coloneqq a^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}_{\mathrm{id}_{Y},c,\mathrm{id}_{X}}
.

One continues by defining the unitary adjoint equivalence for
(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X})_{Y}
using the unitary adjoint equivalence for
a
with the first and last variables held constant. We now provide the tensorator and unitality constraint unitaries
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X}^{2}
and
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X}^{0}
.

(
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X}^{2}
)

We define the unitary tensorator modification
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X}^{2}

to be the following mate of
\pi^{\dagger}
, coming from the pentagonator of
{\mathcal{B}}
.

\pi^{\dagger}
(a\cccirc 1)
a^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}
(1\cccirc a^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}})
a^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}
a^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}

Indeed,
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X}^{2}

is unitary and the unit and counit for the adjoint equivalence

are unitaries.

(
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X}^{0}
)

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X}^{0}

to be the following mate of
\rho
.

\rho
(r^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}\cccirc 1)
a^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}
r^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}

Indeed,
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X}^{2}
is unitary since
\rho
is unitary and the unit and counit for the adjoint equivalence
r
are unitaries

We refer the interested reader to

]
for the proof that

Lemma D.7
.

For a cubical C*-3-category
{\mathcal{B}}
and a 3-morphism
\beta\in{\mathcal{B}}(b\tto b^{\prime})
, there is an organic uniformly bounded perturbation

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{\beta}\colon\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{b}\tttto\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{b^{\prime}}.

This assignment is linear and
{\dagger}
-preserving in
\beta
. Furthermore, when
{\mathcal{B}}
is a cubical W*-3-category, we have that this assignment is normal.

Proof.

We will present the definition found in
[
Gur13
, Lemma 9.10]
, noting that all the relevant data is compatible with dagger structures.

(0)

For an object
A\in{\mathcal{B}}
, we define the component

(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{\beta})_{A}\colon(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{b})_{A}\ttto(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{b^{\prime}})_{A}

to be the uniformly bounded modification given by:

(0.0)

For an object
\mbox{}_{A}{Y}_{B}
, we define the component

((\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{\beta})_{A})_{Y}\colon\underbrace{((\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{b})_{A})_{Y}}_{\mathrm{id}_{Y}\cccirc b}\ttto\underbrace{((\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{b^{\prime}})_{A})_{Y}}_{\mathrm{id}_{Y}\cccirc b^{\prime}}

to be
((\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{\beta})_{A})_{Y}\coloneqq\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{id}_{Y}}\cccirc\beta
.

From this definition it is clear that the assignment
\beta\mapsto\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{\beta}
is linear,
{\dagger}
-preserving, and preserves 3-composition
\circ
and identities at the level of 3-morphisms.
Furthermore, this assignment is normal when
{\mathcal{B}}
is W* by our usual argument using
[
CP22
, Lemma 2.13]
.
We refer the interested reader to
[
Gur13
]
for the proof that
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{\beta}
satisfies the constraint axiom for perturbations.
∎

Theorem D.8
.

We may upgrade the previous data to a monic
{\dagger}
-3-functor

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}\colon{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}({\mathcal{B}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\operatorname{\cccirc\!p}}}\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\Strict}),

which is locally a 2-equivalence.
When
{\mathcal{B}}
is a W*-3-category, we obtain a monic normal
{\dagger}
-3-functor

\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}\colon{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{3Cat}({\mathcal{B}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\operatorname{\cccirc\!p}}}\to{\mathrm{W}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\Strict}).

Proof.

We provide the constraint unitary adjoint equivalences
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{2}
and
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{0}
for the
{\dagger}
-3-functor

found in
[
Gur13
, Theorem 9.12]
.

(
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{2}
)

For composable 1-morphisms
\mbox{}_{B}{X}_{B^{\prime}}
and
\mbox{}_{B^{\prime}}{Y}_{B^{\prime\prime}}
in
cB
, we define the component

to be the
{\dagger}
-3-modification given by:

(
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{2}.0
)

On an object
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
, we define the component

(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{2}_{X,Y})_{A}\colon(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X}\cccirc\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{Y})_{A}\tto(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X\cccirc Y})_{A}

to be the
{\dagger}
-2-natural transformation given by:

(
\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{2}.0.0
)

For an object
\mbox{}_{A}{Z}_{B}\in\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{B}(A)
,

((\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{2}_{X,Y})_{A})_{Z}\colon\underbrace{(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X}\cccirc\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{Y})_{A}(Z)}_{(Z\cccirc X)\cccirc Y}\tto\underbrace{(\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}_{X\cccirc Y})_{A}(Z)}_{Z\cccirc(X\cccirc Y)},

to be

For a 1-morphism

in

\mu
(1\cccirc r^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}})
(\ell^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}\cccirc 1)
a^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\centerdot$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\centerdot$}}}}}

We refer the interested reader to
[
Gur13
]
for the proof that
satisfies both constraint axioms for 3-functors and locally a biequivalence. Quite pedantically, it is also clear that
is injective on every level.
∎

Theorem D.9

(Gelfand-Naimark for operator 3-categories)
.

Every small C*-3-category
{\mathcal{B}}
is 3-equivalent to a sub-C*-Gray-category of
\mathsf{Hilb^{W^{*}2}}
.

Proof.

When
{\mathcal{B}}
is a small
{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Gray}
-category, notice

\displaystyle\includegraphics[scale={.02}]{yo.png}^{\amalg}\colon{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle\to{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\Strict,\Small}

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\mapsto\coprod_{B^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathcal{B}}(B^{\prime}\to B)

is a monic
{\dagger}
-3-functor. Hence, we need only extend the Gelfand-Naimark-Segal construction for C*-2-categories into a monic
{\dagger}
-3-functor

\displaystyle\GNS^{\prime\prime}_{2}\colon{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{2Cat}_{\Strict,\Small}

\displaystyle\to\mathsf{Hilb^{W^{*}2}}.

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\Upsilon_{2}({\mathcal{C}}).

This is done analogously to how one extends the Gelfand-Naimark-Segal construction for C*-1-categories into a monic
{\dagger}
-2-functor

\displaystyle\GNS^{\prime\prime}\colon{\mathrm{C}}^{*}\mathsf{Cat}_{\Small}

\displaystyle\to\mathsf{Hilb^{W^{*}}}.

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\Upsilon({\mathcal{D}}).

We will provide the details for how to define
\GNS^{\prime\prime}_{2}(F)\colon\Upsilon_{2}({\mathcal{C}})\to\Upsilon_{2}({\mathcal{C}}^{\prime})
for a strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}
between strict, small C*-2-categories, and leave the remaining details to the reader. The strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor
\GNS^{\prime\prime}_{2}(F)
maps an object in
\Upsilon_{2}({\mathcal{C}})
given by

\GNS\left(\coprod_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}}{\mathcal{C}}(C^{\prime}\to C)\right)^{\prime\prime}\cong\coprod_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}}\GNS({\mathcal{C}}(C^{\prime}\to C))^{\prime\prime}

to the following object in
\Upsilon_{2}({\mathcal{C}}^{\prime})

\GNS\left(\coprod_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}(C^{\prime}\to FC)\right)^{\prime\prime}\cong\coprod_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}}\GNS({\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}(C^{\prime}\to FC))^{\prime\prime}.

On 1-morphisms and 2-morphisms in
\Upsilon_{2}({\mathcal{C}})
, the action of
\GNS^{\prime\prime}_{2}(F)
is then given by the universal completion of post-composition in each coproduct component. Notice this
{\dagger}
-2-functor is indeed strict since
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}
is a strict C*-2-category and
F
is a strict
{\dagger}
-2-functor.
∎
